## Alfonse Kalazorn

Alfonse Kalazorn, nicknamed "Big Al" for his tall build, was the owner of the Butterskull Ranch in the late 15 century DR.

Possessions
Alfonse Kalazorn owned a suit of mithral chain mail.

History
Alfonse Kalazorn was the sheriff of the town of Triboar until he retired around 1481 DR. As he was bored of retirement, he built up a farm near Conyberry. Big Al's main source of income was the sale of the butter skulls that gave the Butterskull Ranch its name. The butter for these skull-shaped lumps of butter was made from the milk of the cow Petunia.

Circa 1491 DR, the ranch was attacked by a small band of orcs who had been driven from Icespire Hold by the white dragon Cryovain. The orcs freed the pigs and set the barn and the smithy on fire. A few other animals, including the cow Petunia, also escaped. Alfonse and five of his ranch hands tried to defend the ranch but Alfonse was captured and the hands were killed. Only one ranch hand survived and fled on horseback to the village of Phandalin to get help.

## Alkazaar

Alkazaar was a famous 14 century explorer and author who traveled the Realms and wrote about his adventures for others to enjoy.

Description
As described in his writings, Alkazaar was said to be a handsome man who kept a distinguishing moustache. He had impeccable style, choosing to wear loose-fitting shirts, well-polished polished boots, trousers that were a bit-too-tight, and a dashing hat.

Activities
During his life Alkazaar traveled far and wide across Faerûn, earning great acclaim and recognition far and wide. He wrote of his adventures in the massive and widely-beloved autobiography, Alkazaar's Thrilling Tales, and the much-shorter supplemental work Alkazaar's Appendix.

History
After many years of travels, Alkazaar eventually set out on one final adventurer, traveling to Anauroch in order to recover one of the fabled Nether Scrolls. While traversing the desert, Alkazaar encountered an ancient stone golem, gaining its trust and a new traveling companion. The explored garnered a possible location of interest from some of the desert's native Bedine people, but the expedition was halted when his traveling group was dispersed by a devastating sandstorm.

In the, Alkazaar traveled to Candlekeep on the Sword Coast and gained admittance into the library-fortress with copies of his famous autobiography, and its much shorter addition. He regaled the monks of the Avowed with stories of his travels, the memories of which many of them cherished for decades.

Within the appendix of his famous book, Alkazaar included a magical picture portal that could be activated by the right individuals, along with coded command words that would allow for its activation.

## Al'chaia

Al'chaia was the leading githyanki commander in Undermountain in the early 1490s DR. She was the ruler of Stardock until succeeded by Commander Urlon.

History
Sometime in the late 15 century DR,  Al'chaia led a group of githyanki into the Crystal Labyrinth while tracking down a colony of mind flayers in the Seadeeps level. Upon discovery of the portal leading to Stardock, they converted the abandoned asteroid into a crèche for the breeding and training of young githyanki, which they named Crèche K'liir. The labyrinth was converted into a fortress from where they led attacks against the mind flayers and protected access to Stardock.

Around the Year of Three Ships Sailing, 1492 DR, Al'chaia resided in Stardock.

Activities
Al'chaia was in her quarters studying the manual of gainful exercise or the tome of clear thought she had recently found.

Relationships
Al'chaia's mount was the loyal Zolcharrx, one of the daughters of Ashtyrranthor, who was her main ally. Her second-in-command Urlon plotted against her.

Personality
Al'chaia was very cruel to her subordinates. She knew how to motivate her soldiers, by promising them the two magic books. Al'chaia was worried about the githzerai Ezria her soldiers had recently captured, fearing that more would arrive.

## Alzarro Rumnthorygh

Alzarro Rumnthorygh was the proprietor of Rumnthorygh's Welcoming House inn in Mosstone, Tethyr, in the 14 century DR.

History
Alzarro Rumnthorygh was a dedicated innkeeper. His establishment was renowned for its cheerful atmosphere and cheap prices. Unfortunately, the tiny inn was always jam-packed with customers, turning the place into chaos with kitchens unable to keep up with orders. In addition, the building was so cramped, Alzarro had to sleep in stables' rafters. Eventually, Alzarro Rumnthorygh was able to expand his inn by building a new wing in the inn's back. The expansion was welcomed, but it also rose prices at the inn to recoup the expenses.

## Alatian's Spell Shroud

Alatian's Spell Shroud was an eerily-glowing piece of enchanted clothing that belonged to Alatian, a servant and an apprentice of a lich named Estera.

Powers
This set of magical clothing possessed a +1 enchantment to the wearer's defense and it emitted dim blue lights that illuminated  around it. When the wearer of Alatian's Spell Shroud was struck, the item unleashed a burst of dispelling magic on the attacker. The wearer also received access to the spell sequencer spell stored within the robe. This spell was accessible once per day.

History
Sometime before 1372 DR, these magical robes were gifted to Alatian by his undead master. The lich's apprentice was often sent on accusation mission of various items from the land the living by Estera. During these expeditions, those who witnessed Alatian creeping through graveyards late at night, assumed that the eery glow radiating from the Spell Shroud was a restless ghost. The Shroud remained in Alatian's possession until he himself ascended into lichdom.

Alatian's Spell Shroud eventually ended up in possession of Boonmoppet's master. The wizard, and later a lich, was killed before 1372 DR, leaving his tower and his magical goods in the hands of the lonely imp familiar.

## Aldrine Lugar

Aldrine Lugar was a somewhat famous sailor who survived being stranded on a small island in the Sea of Swords and floated back to Daggerford on a giant gourd, which later came to be known as the Castaway Gourd.

History
Aldrine Lugar's ship was caught in a storm. She ended up being swept off deck and woke up on a small rocky island. The island was almost barren, lacking any means of survival, apart from some blueberry plants. The sailor consumed all the berries within the first couple of days and soon resigned to her fate of starving to death alone. The only company on the island she had were fluttering colorful butterflies that she came to see as a symbol of freedom. Her salvation was washed ashore one night—a giant floating gourd from the swamps of the Lizard Marsh. Aldrine knew of the Marsh's lizardfolk and their enormous gourd flask and decided to tempt the good graces of Umberlee. She carved the word "butterfly" on the colorful gourd, attached herself with vines from the island, and set off into the Sea of Swords. Three days later, she found herself washed ashore not far from the Delimbiyr River's neck.

Eventually, Aldrine Lugar's tale became popular among seafarring folk of Daggerford, and the famous gourd was proudly displayed in Torleth's Treasures, the curio shops of Gillian's Hill circa 1372 DR.

He was absolutely fair and honorable in his dealings, though he heeded no excuses nor pleas of extenuating circumstances.  However, circumstances could draw him towards desperate measures, such as framing adventurers of mischief in order to force them into a quest.

Alham was a less-than-adept fighter. He also presumably had multiple means of execution open to him, including death by fireball, death by lightning, death by flame strike, and death by polymorphing, suggesting that he employed several wizards and perhaps priests.

Alham Pasha Burak's dagger was valued at 250 gp.

As the Hetmann, his word was law in the caravanserai. The Durpari Airlancers and Lancers carried out his orders. He was aware of multiple rumors which he could not investigate fully.

Hetmann Alham Pasha Burak was friends with the wizard Aman Al-Raqib.

 In Interior Faerûn, alfalfa was a staple crop in the nations of Cormyr and Sembia. In Cormyr it was one of the grains that filled fields across the nation that spanned hundreds of acres, including the Kirinwood. And in Sembia, the typical farm had dedicated field plots to alfalfa.

## Al-Alisk

The Al-Alisk clan were desert dwellers living close to the southern end of Raurin. They eventually died off to the Curse of Amun-Re, and a blood feud with the people of Akhir and Pazar; however, they continued to rise nightly upon death to do battle with these enemies.

Overview
At some point in the late 3 century DR, Sithal Al-Husn, a princess of Pazar, was kidnapped from the streets of Akhir on her wedding night by a man named Habrauk Al-Nirin, of the Al-Alisk clan. This set off a blood feud which, combined with the Curse of Amun-Re, completely wiped out the bloodlines of the Al-Alisk clan, the city of Akhir, and the royal line of Pazar within five generations.

The Al-Alisk Clan buried their dead in the Crypt of Al-Alisk, which was approximately  east by northeast of Akhir. Even after the destruction of the clan, they rose nightly to guard the crypt against the Lancers of Death until the year, when adventurers released the frozen princess of Pazar.

## Allison Chisolm

Allison Chisolm was a baker at the family-run Downunda Patisserie in Ravens Bluff in the Vast in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Relationships
She was the daughter of Jemima and Arvend Chisolm, and the middle sister of Derek and Karl.

Personality
Allison was kind and friendly to everyone, and while she understood how beautiful she was, she wasn't conceited or vain. She loved her family and loved to spoil Karl. Unlike her mother and brothers, she was willing to talk about her father, but only in confidence and when her family weren't around.

While she'd had no chance to get formal schooling, her experiences made her wiser than others her age. Allison was interested in magic and yearned for adventure, especially if good-looking young men were involved. There was a good chance she'd run off with a band of young fellow adventures to become an adventurer herself some day, possibly as a thief or magic-user, with proper training. But her mother feared this and disapproved of adventurers.

Description
At age 17, Allison stood  and weighed . She had flame-red hair and eyes that matched her mother's bright blue, and strikingly beautiful. Her mother rationed her and her siblings to only three of the bakery's smaller cakes a week to prevent them putting on weight.

Activities
She lived above the store. Allison was kept busy helping her mother in the bakery by putting out and selling goods and working second shifts at the ovens, and running the store when Jemima was busy.

History
When her father Arvend was executed for murder and all the family's belongings seized, leaving them destitute and ashamed, Allison left with her family to build a new life. They arrived in Ravens Bluff and purchased the bakery. She was still working there by 1370 DR.

## Alicine Bricket

Alicine Bricket was a spoiled and money-loving noblewoman from Daggerford, sister to Muriel and Elveronne Bricket in the 14 century DR.

Description
Alicine wan an older human woman with a neatly affixed hairstyle, pulled back to expose her broad forehead.

Personality
Alicine had a submissive personality, dominated by her sister. She valued her family's traditions and customs. Her handwriting was sophisticated, and she tended to write in flowery prose. Her hard leather found and decorated journal was doused in the pleasant stench of perfume.

Relationships
Alicine's good friend, Sancha, hailed from the town of Bowshot. Since early childhood, they had been penpals and remained close while remaining physically apart through most of their lives.

History
Like her sister, Alicine openly feuded with their brother over his strict money management and tight control of the Bricket family estate. In 1372 DR, Muriel Bricket hatched a plot to poison the old man and take over the family fortune without Alicine's knowledge.

As a final act of defiance during the funeral, Alicine bent down to Elveronne's body, pretending to pay respects. With a quick sleight of hand, she pocketed the man's beloved enchanted elven keepsake necklace, the item she longed for many decades. She cherished taking the thing Elveronne loved the most in life and planned on enjoying the taste of revenge daily by looking at the intricate piece of stolen jewelry. However, that act had fateful consciences.

Soon after Elveronne's death, Muriel and Alicine organized a lavish party to celebrate their freedom. The sisters invited several of their friends, including Sancha, all of whom shared disdain for the old man. However, during the celebration, Elveronne Bricket rose from the Bricket family crypt and shambled towards the Bricket Homestead house in the Western Farmlands. The corpse's disappearance prompted the individual later known as the Hero of Daggerford to investigate the strange occurrence. This led the Hero and their companions to the odd celebration. Once sisters realized the Hero of Daggerford investigated the death of their "beloved" brother, both excused themselves from the party and headed to the second floor of the manor, attempting to hide any evidence there might be of the murder. Alicine feverishly tossed her diary into a locked chest inside her room and, when confronted with her suspicious actions, claimed that she merely organized old papers. However, the Hero clearly heard a thump of a heavy hardbound book.

Alicine's journal revealed to the Hero that she stole Elveronne's beloved keepsake - the act that was the likely reason for his return from the grave. Upon further investigation, the Hero discovered Muriel's murder weapon, a potent poison, revealing the true culprit. When confronted with the truth, Alicine was horrified by her sister's actions and her own celebrations of the old man's death. She wished to trade places with Elveronne, as his death was unjust.

The Hero discovered Elveronne Bricket in his undead form, burning with palpable hatred and murder lust, shuffling around the manor's basement. Ignoring the creature's revenant form, the Hero communicated with Elveronne and discovered that the undead wanted revenge or his keepsake returned. However, it was unknown if the Hero poured the holy water on Elveronne, destroying him, negotiated the return of the enchanted elven keepsake necklace, or allowed old man Bricket to exact his murderous revenge. One thing was clear - in the end, Elveronne Bricket was returned to eternal rest, one way or another.

Neverwinter Nights: Darkness over Daggerford

## Aleithilithos

Aleithilithos was a great wyrm amethyst dragon and a thane of the gem dragon god Sardior.

Abilities
Aleithilithos possessed all the common powers of amethyst dragons and was a master of psionics, focusing on psychokinesis. In particular, he knew the powers detection of good or evil, detection of magic, dimension walk, energy control, and  object reading and all the psionic attacks (mind thrust, ego whip, id insinuation, psychic crush, mind blast) and defenses (empty mind, thought shield, mental barrier, intellect fortress, tower of iron will).

As a thane, Aleithilithos had a continuous mental contact with Sardior and could be used for his remote viewing, though he could choose to block it.

Activities
Aleithilithos and the other thanes normally resided within Sardior's Ruby Palace as part of his court, but on occasion would wander a world of the material plane alone. Creatures that earned his favor might be rewarded with gems, or sometimes some other gift or favor. He represented a certain facet of Sardior's personality.

On worlds where Sardior was worshiped, some 10% of later temples to him were dedicated to Aleithilithos. At these temples, lesser in size and overlooked places, Aleithilithos was revered by the faithful and served as the messenger and conduit of their faith. They attracted psions specializing in psychokinesis. There were also small shrines alongside major Sardioran churches in larger cities. However, aleithian dwarves dedicated their temples specifically to Aleithilithos.

Relationships
Aleithilithos served Sardior as his thane, alongside fellow thanes the crystal dragon Hrodel, the emerald dragon Smargad, the sapphire dragon Charisma, the topaz dragon Tithonnas, and the destroyed obsidian dragon Seradess.

History
Once, Aleithilithos was set a task by Sardior, and went deep underground of some world to contemplate it. Eventually, he was interrupted when a dig team of the deep dwarves of Rokehold inadvertently broke through into his temporary lair. They ran in fright, but Aleithilithos was only amused as he realized that, while he had not planned on company, they were the solution to his problem.

Aleithilithos made his proposal to Sardior, who accepted it and negotiated with the dwarf god Moradin with Dumathoin as an intermediary. Moradin permitted the Rokehold dwarves to serve Sardior as his caretakers. Sardior paid Moradin back with a few secrets of his foes. Agents of Sardior granted the Rokehold dwarves a portal to the Demiplane of Ectoplasm and training in constructing stable mines there, the use of ectoplasm, and cutting crystals. In exchange, the dwarves were expected to defend the demiplane against trespassers. The Rokehold dwarves explored and mined the demiplane eagerly.

In the centuries that followed, the Rokehold dwarves dug an extensive tunnel network in the Demiplane of Ectoplasm, but grew more apart from their kind. Meanwhile, their physiology adapted to suit their environment and acquired innate psionic talents. This was Sardior's influence via Aleithilithos and they worshiped Aleithilithos directly. Thus they became the aleithian dwarves, after Aleithilithos.

Eventually, some time in the mid-to-late 12 century DR, by Toril time, Aleithilithos returned to the Rokehold. He commended the dwarves for their work, gave them some additional instruction, and sealed the portal, while advising them to ready themselves for a return to the surface, where their new gifts would soon be needed. They began a migration to the surface of their world and to meet other races.

## Alasalynn Rowanmantle

Lady Regent Alasalynn Rowanmantle was a skilled warrior and Purple Dragon during the mid–14 century DR, and served as the ruler of the Cormyrean protectorate of Tilverton until the city's destruction in 1372 DR.

Activities
Lady Regent Rowanmantle was elected to her position as Lady of Tilverton by the city's council of citizens, which she took the leadership of.

Relationships
Alasalynn was the most prominent member of the Rowanmantle family and cousin of Shaerl Amcathra. She was served well by Cuthric Snow, a herald appointed to her by the Cormyrean court. She was on good terms with Barrit, a local scholar of Tilverton.

History
Before becoming regent of the city, Alasalynn served as an officer within the Purple Dragons.

Shortly after Cormyr's annexation of Tilverton in 1357 DR, she was elected as Lady Regent, replacing the brief, heavy-handed, and unpopular rule of Duke Bhereu Ammaeth. She proved to be a much more popular ruler of the protectorate.

In the, Lady Alasalynn attended King Azoun Obarskyr IV's birthday feast, hosted by Lord Partic Thistle at Thistleflame Keep. She was accompanied there by Lord Damaris Rowanmantle.

When the shades of Thultanthar returned to Faerûn in the, it became apparent to Princess Alusair Obarskyr that they were a threat to all of Cormyr. On Mirtul 27, Alusair toured Tilverton with her court mages Vangerdahast Aeiulvana and Caladnei, along with Lady Regent Rowanmantle and an elf from Evereska named Galaeron Nihmedu. As they came to believe Tilverton would be the first target of the Princes of Shade, Princess Alusair tasked the Lady Regent with the evacuation of her city.

Soon after, Vangerdahast let loose a powerful magical weapon upon the shade army, which ultimately resulted in the complete destruction of Tilverton. In the aftermath of the disaster, it appeared as if Lady Rowanmantle had been quite successful in getting her people safely out the city.

## Alamarayne Moonray

Alamarayne Moonray was a priestess of Selûne residing in Elmwood. She assisted Dorn Graybrook and Karasendrieth in repelling the Zhentilar that occupied her village in the .

Description
Alamarayne was a beauty, and she had a pure laugh, described as "like liquid moonlight" in 1367 DR. When the party led by Dorn Graybrook found her in 1373 DR, she was described as slender, and she dressed in the silver robes of a priestess of Selûne.

Abilities
Alamarayne had employed divinations to determine the capabilities of the Zhentilar forces occupying Elmwood, ascertaining that they may have divinatory powers of their own. When fighting the Zhents, she employed such spells as hold person and dismissal.

Personality
Alamarayne did not flaunt her beauty. She had a mischievous streak. According to Mendryll Belarod, she was wise too. Per Will Turnstone, she was merry and even coquettish, though she could be as cheery as a mass grave sometimes.

Activities
In  Alamarayne was the priestess at the Temple of the Half Moon of Elmwood. In that year, she also met once a week with Thoyana Jorgadaul at the Still Waters for conversation, dinner, and a game of chess, but those were different meetings than the ones with Ezril. In, once a week, she, Thoyana and Ezril Treewarder met to discuss the state of the town.

Relationships
In, Alamarayne Moonray spent a few pleasant evenings adeptly researchcing the effects of moonlight on romantic situations with Mendryll Belarod. She eventually stood him up just as he was going to tell her about noises he heard coming from the village well.

History
In, she was assisted by Dorn Graybrook's party in ridding Elmwood of the occupying force sent from Zhentil Keep in a conquering bid intended to take advantage of the Rage of Dragons between Tarsakh 11 and Tarsakh 12.

## Alaodin

Alaodin was an assassin and follower of Bhaal. He ran an assassins' guild from Sentinelspire until he was supplanted by Chereth. He was also Talieth's father.
Appearance
When Chereth disguised himself as Alaodin, he took on the appearance of an old man in a black, hooded robe. His face had a manly chin, his hair was thinning and gray, but with streaks of black, just up to his shoulders. He had the darker skin tone of a man from the southern regions, intensely wrinkled, but with bright and sharp eyes.
History
Per his daughter, Talieth, early life for Alaodin was not easy: he was more often surviving than living. He eventually became, however, a man of wealth and power, and a follower of the god Bhaal. He took residence in a fortress hidden in the mountain of Sentinelspire, where, armed with divine inspiration, he created a garden where every pleasure was at hand for his followers, assassins he sent across the land and put in the service of the rich and powerful. He often sought as payment official sanction for the worship of Bhaal.
During this time, Alaodin gathered a number of killers to himself, including an orphan from Elversult, Kheil, and a tribal half-orc, Sauk. After the death of Bhaal, he began looking feverishly for other sources of power, including relics, lore, and allies. All in all, there were about fifty followers of the Old Man by, including some wizards.
Around, Chereth reached out to Alaodin through contacts in Glarondar, requesting an audience to study the Imaskari fortress of Sentinelspire. The Old Man of the Mountain granted him the request, presumably hoping that the druid would uncover something he could use, and he spent days and nights studying the relics in the fortress, until he came across Erael'len, which he stole, slaying several assassins while escaping. In retaliation, Alaodin sent his best assassins, including Kheil and Sauk, after Chereth and his allies in the Yuirwood on . The attempt on Chereth's life was unsuccessful.
By, Alaodin had put down two insurrections, as the death of his god in  and Chereth's escape in  had far reduced his standing. The second insurrection, led by his daughter Talieth, had led to the death of almost half of the Blades of the Old Man, and left the others far from fighting shape. That year, Chereth returned and slew Alaodin, impersonating him thereafter.

## Alvira's stasis shell

Alvira's Stasis Shell was a very rare spell that Alvira created to protect objects.

Effects
The spell placed a faint blue glow upon the object that it was cast upon, which had to be between the sizes of a potion vial and a normal spellbook; the object became impossible to affect with anything short of disintegrate or a successful dispel magic spell. Though the object could be moved normally, unlike in stronger stasis spells, it was impossible to affect it much. Most attacks, even dragon breath, would simply slide off around the protected item. Though it could not affect living tissue directly, there had been reports of it working on living tissue inside an inanimate container. For all intents and purposes, time had stopped for the object within the field.

Components
Besides verbal and somatic components, the spell involved the protected object in its magic.Though the spell is supposed to have a material component, neither source speaks of what it is.

History
The spell had been developed by Alvira, a hedge wizard of Raven's Bluff; it was very rare and even more so outside of hedge mages. She kept a copy of her spellbook protected with this spell and a permanency, just in case something were to happen to her working book.The original says that she hid her spellbook combining this spell with extension; this is considered to be an error in this article, as no extension spell extant at the time of publishing could affect 6th level spells.

## Alchemical sleep gas

Alchemical sleep gas was a substance used to render someone unconscious.

Description
The gas was stored in liquid form in sealed containers, but it was extremely volatile and would form a gas at room temperature when released from its container. Alchemists of the Realms often sold the substance in sealed glass flasks that could be used like grenade weapons. These flasks weighed roughly .

Only mages highly trained in the skill of alchemy could synthesize sleep gas. Flasks of the stuff sold in most markets for about 30 gold pieces a piece.

Powers
At high concentrations, the gas was slightly toxic and would put living creatures to sleep, usually for about only one minute but perhaps as long as five minutes. The toxin could even knock unconscious such creatures like elves who were immune to magical sleeping spells, but it did not work as well or at all on creatures that were resistant or immune to poisons, respectively. The cloud of gas formed from a typical flask was only potent over an area of about  square and quickly dissipated.

History
The thief Gage from Laothkund carried a dose of alchemical sleep gas in a bottle labeled Inhalant as of .

Aloe vera plants were tropical curative herbs found in the Malatran jungles.

Juice of aloe vera plants could be easily extracted from the leaves and used as a topical ointment. The juice protected the user's skin from irritation. This plant also allowed creatures like lizardfolk to keep their skin wet longer without daily baths.

In the 14 century DR, aloe vera plant were known to be gifted to goodly travelers by the lesser nature spirits of Malatran vanilla grove Timon and Pumba.

## Allium plant

Allium plants were curative herbs found in the Malatran jungles.

Description
Allium was a hardy onion-like root vegetable plant that reached  in length. It was commonly found on the Malatran Plateau as well as the desert of Anauroch. Both root bulb and long green asparagus-like spears were edible and possessed curative properties. Even when dead, allium plants retained their properties, as was evident by them never rotting nor breeding any fungus nor molds. When eaten whole, it protected the imbiber from poisons and diseases, or it reduced the duration of such afflictions.

Inhabitants of Anauroch, both humanoids and beasts, consumed allium as a good source of always precious water.

History
In the 14 century DR, allium plants were known to be gifted to goodly travelers by the lesser nature spirits of Malatran vanilla grove Timon and Pumba.

## Allinzir Gi'Leera

Allinzir Gi'leera was a proxy of Vhaeraun, the Dark Seldarine deity of thievery and treachery. He was also the sole individual among Vhaeraun's proxies — who for their own safety tried to avoid attention and keep their identities secret — that was publicly known.

Description
Like all of Vhaeraun's proxies, Allinzir was a male drow.

Personality
Allinzir was an incredibly subtle and constantly scheming individual. He carried himself with an air of smooth pleasantness and a cold smile that almost never faltered. He always looked for indirect or roundabout solutions to any problem he faced, even if a straightforward one was available.

Abilities
Allinzir possessed the same abilities that were given to all of Vhaeraun's proxies. This included trusight, a catlike grace, abilities related to thievery, and abilities related to striking at creatures that could only be harmed by magical weapons.

Unlike other drow, Allinzir was uniquely unaffected by sunlight. And he could disappear into the shadows, even while being watched, then reappear elsewhere.

Activities
Allinzir operated in the shadows, working against the machinations of the Dark Seldarine goddess Lolth, just like all proxies of Vhaeraun. Beyond this duty as Vhaeraun's proxy, Allinzir operated as a member of the planar faction known as the Revolutionary League.

## Alyssa

Alyssa was a high priestess of Umberlee who oversaw the Temple of Umberlee in the Thayan city of Bezantur in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
She was dedicated to the greater glory of her goddess, but was also known to enrich herself in the process of collecting Umberlee's tribute.

Activities
Alyssa oversaw the more than one hundred priests, priestesses, and acolytes of Umberlee who staffed the temple, and presided over the temple's practice of recruiting thugs to steal treasure from ships in port in order to pay tribute to her goddess and to enrich the staff themselves. She was known to use temple funds to host lavish parties each tenday which attracted the upper echelons of Bezantur society, even including Zulkir Aznar Thrul and his retinue. The fact that Umberlee allowed this was seen as a sign of the goddess's generosity toward Alyssa.

## Alorgoth

Alorgoth, also known by the alias of Collus Darathon, was a servant of Shar and the founder of the Dark Moon monastic order that arose within the Sharran faith during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
He was a tall and slender man with a pointed chin.

Personality
Alorgoth was a ruthless and sadistic man, wholly dedicated to Shar and her dogma. He had no compassion for recruits of his order that did not pass their trials, and even found pleasure in personally carrying out their executions.

Abilities
He was very skilled at altering his appearance and voice while acting under one of his aliases.

When pressed in battle, he was not above summoning demons to his aid.

Possessions
Among Alorgoth's possessions were a Cloak of the Dark Moon, a Headband of the Devout, and his pair of Night Walkers. Interestingly enough for a servant of Shar, he kept a journal outlying some secrets pertaining operations carried out by the Dark Moon monks.

Activities

Alorgoth spent much of his time traveling across east Faerûn to the Lands of Intrigue, spreading Shar's control while cavorting with corrupt rulers and unscrupulous nobles. He manipulated their deepest secrets and most cherished memories as his weapons, and destroyed damning information as needed to shield Shar's will from scrutiny. He spread fear amongst the populace with every secret expertly disseminated, increasing Shar's influence over their collective psyche.

He often employed different aliases, such as of Collus Darathon the founder of the Twofold Trust. Using these assumed personas Alorgoth has sparked many conflicts, outright wars, and pit many close friends and loved ones against one another.

After founding the Order of the Dark Moon, Alorgoth directed them to eradicate followers of Selûne, specifically monks of the Order of the Sun Soul.

Relationships
While Alorgoth and his monks operated independently of the Sharran church, he commanded great authority among the faithful. He was said by some to be Shar's most favored and trusted servant in the Realms.

He had an ally in Yxtrazzal, a shadow dragon that was in possession of the Wheel of Obliteration.

History
Alorgoth received a holy directive from Shar to found the Order of the Dark Moon, with three distinct criteria: all recruits would be young adults, with no previous affiliation with the Sharran clergy, and most importantly, each had to demonstrate distinct sorcerous abilities.

He traveled from eastern Faerûn to Amn, and established the first monastery of his order in the city of Purskul in the . He then toured the cities of the realm, passing through Keczulla, Crimmor, and even the capital of Athkatla in search of potential recruits for Shar's new order. It was there in the City of Coin that Alorgoth discovered Gamaz, a young Sun Soul monk that would convert to the worship of Shar and lead the Dark Moon monastery in the Cloud Peaks.

After finding about a dozen individuals that could fulfill Shar's will, Alorgoth returned to Purskul with his new pupils, to carry begin their years-long course of training.

While still in Amn, Alorgoth exploited the emergent Dark Moon heresy to form a new cult of the "Twofold Truth" that attracted worshipers of Selûne away from her church. Using the alias of Collus Darathon, he gathered the Selûnites—along with novice Sharrans that veered away from her sole worship—at the Temple of the Twofold Goddess to be slaughtered outright by his more experienced Dark Moon monks. Fortunately for the novice monks of both faiths, the attack was thwarted by the Sun Soul monk Rasaad yn Bashir, his ally Abdel Adrian, and their traveling companions.

Later that year, Alorgoth then ventured to Mount Deepstone with a cell of Sharrans that had enslaved the dwarves of Clan Deepstone. The Sharrans forced the Deepstone dwarves to dig down to a cluster of shadow crystals, which they could then use to open a portal to the Shadowfell by utilizing the Wheel of Obliteration.

Some time around the,The 3 edition sourcebook Lords of Darkness states this occurred in 1370 DR, while the 3.5 edition book Champions of Ruin states this occurred in 1371 DR. Alorgoth turned operations of the temple over to clergy of Shar, and he and the first strike out at Shar's enemies across the Realms. They influence spread all the way from their home in Amn, north to Baldur's Gate, all the way to to Cormyr in the Eastern Heartlands.

## Alia Shield-Maiden

Alia Shield-Maiden was the wife of Halgren Death's Head and a fellow member of the adventuring company – Annals of Halgren circa the 13 century DR.

Description
Alia Shield-Maiden was a stout, strong, and well-armored beardless dwarven woman. Her usual weapons of choice were an axe and a heavy shield. She wore a horned helmet. Alia accepted the fact that the career she chose was a bloody one, and she changed her religion accordingly. She kept a detailed journal that chronicled the adventures of the Annals of Halgren.

History
Alia and her husband hailed from the dwarven Bruenghor Clan of the Sunset Mountains. The duo established their adventuring group circa the  in order to gain wealth, fame, and reputation big enough to overshadow Halgren's disgraced family past. While adventuring with the Annals of Halgren, Alia abandoned the dwarven pantheon and adopted the faith in Tempus, the Lord of Battles. She used her clerical gifts to aid her husband in battle and heal his wounds. The name "Shield-Maiden" referred to Alia's place in battle – to Halgren's right side, his shield arm.

In the, Annals of Halgren found itself in the northern city of Luskan. By that time, the troupe gained new members, Bastinado, Silence-Before-Iron, Mordakai of Thay, and Kei the Wooden Idol.

## Allspice

Allspice, also called pimento or everry, was an expensive spice produced by a berry-bearing bush.

Availability
In the Cormyrean city of Gladehap, Wyrmkindler Sausage Works used one thruppinch of allspice berries per  of stag meat in preparation for their ever-popular stag sausages.

Elves of the ancient city of Myth Drannor in the forest of Cormanthor were quite fond of allspice-seasoned pumpkin soup. A single whiff of the soup evoked memories of a legend about Aerdrie Faenya, which told of her transforming the heads of slain orcs into pumpkins. The story symbolized elves using orcs' heads more effectively than they ever did themselves.

Bakers of the wizardly nation of Halruaa used sugar, cardamom, mace, and allspice mixture to dust freshly fried sweet buns and hangman's nooses.

Dwarves of the Northkingdom used allspice to prepare sweet, savory, and spicy Tide-Me-Overs.

Allspice, or pimento, was sold across the Realms via Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue, priced at 40 gold pieces per .

On the Malatran Plateau in Kara-Tur, allspice was used as a pickling spice, together with mustard seeds, black peppercorns, red chiles, cloves, coriander, ginger, and mace.

Usages
Allspice berries could be used to create enchanted oil that could improve one's strength. A vial of such oil could cost up to 750 gp.

History
In the, Gravia's hot apple cider, prepared by Gravia Maxillian of Daggerford's farmlands, called for two allspice berries per pot. Secretly a werewolf, Gravia drugged her cider with Zakharan knockout drops to capture unwitting travelers to be hunted by her bloodthirsty family.

An alchemical lightThe device is never given a formal name. However, the narration calls it an "alchemical light", and thus, that is the name chosen in this article. was an alchemical device. It contained two separate liquids in a glass vessel; when stirred vigorously, these liquids mixed together, creating a faint light. ## Albatross

Albatrosses were a species of seafaring birds found around Faerûn.

Description
The average albatross measured  in length and exhibited a wingspan of up to  in length.

Abilities
These creatures were capable of seeing in areas of low-light and they were capable of flying without rest for incredible lengths of time.

Combat
Beyond creatures they were preying upon, albatrosses generally did not attack unless they were being threatened by a creature they could not easily escape from.

Ecology
Diet
These birds were a carnivorous species, subsisting primarily upon fish and squids. They sometimes trailed behind ships in order to feed upon the refuse that was dumped overboard.
Habitat
Albatrosses were typically found in areas near the sea that had a warm climate, but could also be found quite far away from land.
Relationships
Albatrosses were a favored animal of Valkur, a Faerûnian deity of the ocean, and acted as his servants. Sometimes, Valkur would send albatrosses to appear before worshipers to indicate his favor.

Some sorcerers kept albatrosses as familiars, while some spirit shamans had them as a spirit guide.

Trivia
 The Beached Leviathan, an inn and tavern in the city of Neverwinter, used "albatross" as a slang term to refer to someone that was bringing bad luck to the establishment.
 These birds were the namesake for the Hooked Albatross Inn of Ravens Bluff.

Appendix
External Links

## Allisa

Allisa of the Mists was a powerful human paladin and a "mistrider" or "mistwalker," active in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Allisa was a talented combatant and a devoted follower of her deity. While she used her strength to fight, she never hesitated to shield members of her church from harm. She was known for her gentle and caring personality that contrasted with her strong exterior. She spoke little.

Possessions
Allisa was known to wear a full set of enchanted +2 plate armor as well as a magical +3 shield which bolstered her in combat. Additionally, she wielded a scimitar of speed and a helm of telepathy, giving her the advantage in any fight.

Abilities
Allisa of the Mists gained her nickname for her uncanny ability to pass through any mist, along with her unicorn mount, into any of the Domains of Dread she chose. She was even more rarely defeated than she spoke.

Activities
The paladin was known to arrive always with the morning mists.

History
At some point, Allisa's trusted mount was a unicorn named Skuldreir. Later, the creature was replaced by another unicorn named Cissl who found the paladin on its own accord and the duo were inseparable ever since.

Rumors & Legends
Sages had debated that the mysterious heroine may have been a minor avatar of Lathander. A few claimed she must've been tied to the lands of Ravenloft.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

## Alahandra's questing call

Alahandra's questing call was a spell that Alahandra created to call adventurers to a quest.

Effects
The spell called forth the vague shadowy images of adventurers within  who were not on a quest at the moment, as well as their general location for at least 15 minutes, more for more advanced mages. The spellcaster had little control over which adventurers the spell showed; about three out of every ten times, the spell showed nothing, or scenes from other planes.

Components
Besides verbal and somatic components, the spell called for a silver mirror, a candle made of beeswax, and a small bell. Of these three material components, only the mirror remained after casting.

History
The spell had been developed by Alahandra, a hedge wizard of Waterdeep. The spell was quite rare, and less than one in thirty wizards outside of hedge wizards bothered to learn it.

Appendix
References

---

## Alaoreum River

Alaoreum River was a river that began in the Mountains of the Alaoreum, ran south along the shoreline of the Sea of Fallen Stars, and ended in Evenstar Lake.

History
The flooding of the Alaoreum River became ruinous for the settlement of Morningstar Hollows. It took several seasons for the land to become consumed by swamps and the inhabitants of Morningstar Hollows moved to the nearby town of Velorn's Valor. By the late-14 century DR, the village was believed to be inhabited by a three dozen lizardfolk who claimed the abandoned human lands.

In the, druids of Eldath and the Emerald Enclave clashed with undead armies of mohrg warlord Borran Klosk in service of Malar. The Battle of Morningstar Hollows concluded in the druids rising waters of Alaoreum River, flooding the undead and, eventually, trapped the mohrg beneath Alaghôn.

Borran Klosk was released from his prison in the . Morningstar Hollows were taken by the creature's undead armies once more. Klosk used his necromancy to full his army with long-dead folk, murdered by the Emerald Enclave.

Over the centuries since Ironcloak's ruination, just to the east from the river, numerous adventurers searched for the treasure cache of the town's lord. Alaoreum River was believed to hide the drowned cache. The treasure rained undiscovered as of the late-14 century DR.

Notable Locations
Evenstar Lake, a scenic lake a the end of the river, located in the Evenstar Vale, west from Alaghôn.
Morningstar Hollows, a human fishing village, abandoned to bogs and flooding.
Swordslake Creek, a small human mining hamlet on the edge of Alaoreum Mountains.

Appendix
Appearances
Novels
The Jewel of Turmish

References

---

## Alanaerle

Alanaerle was the proprietor of the Weary Knight Inn in Arabel, Cormyr, in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Alanaerle was a large human woman with a personality just as big. She was considered unattractive, and her face was said to have scared children. Alanaerle was always jolly and hospitable. She was a lover of romantic tales and ballads with happy endings, often giving half-off discounts to traveling bards and minstrels in exchange for a performance.

Possessions
Alanaerle's prized possession was her roadhouse – the Weary Knight Inn. The inn was possibly the most average establishment in the Realms, according to Volothamp Geddarm. The Knight's namesake was an unopened tomb of a knight that dated to times of antiquity. Alanaerle guarded the carved tomb and its undiscovered contents with passion.

Relationships
The inn's proprietor was devoted to the Lady Lord of Arabel, Myrmeen Lhal, and provided free lodgings and food for those in possession of the Lady Lord's special black onyx rings.

History
By the, Alanaerle was already the proprietor of the Weary Knight Inn that stood directly to the east from the Citadel and three blocks away from the city's Eastgate. In 1360 DR, Alanaerle provided food and room for a small group of adventurers tasked by Myrmeen Lhal with the investigation into a series of wizard murders in Arabel.

As of the, Alanaerle and her establishment remained open and popular. Volothamp Geddarm listed the inn in his Guide to Cormyr and complemented Alanaerle for her pleasant attitude.

Appendix
Appearances
Adventures
Dungeon #61: "Storm Season"

References

---

## Alataz Thrindol

Alataz Thrindol was a merchant from the city of Telflamm who once resided in the small town of Ravilar's Cloak in Turmish.

Description
Alataz was a grim-looking man with a shaved head. The merchant was renowned for nerves of steel and sharp blades that discouraged his competitors from crossing the man.

History
Before settling in the Ravilar's Cloak, Alataz Thrindol had a rich career and many travels under his belt. Alataz spent about a dozen years at the Cloak before embarking on another journey.

Circa the, Ravilar's Cloak's inhabitants discovered a bag of emeralds valued at more than 686,000 gold coins. The discovery was made in a drained cesspit near Alataz Thrindol's house. The locals quickly assumed that the treasure belonged to the wealthy merchant who's been gone from his home in the Clock for seven years at the time of discovery.

Alataz's fate remained unknown as his home was claimed by Rastar of the Blades, a factor from Calimshan.

Appendix
References

---

## Alesam Mischwin

Alesam Mischwin was a merchant prince and the Lord of Turmish in the city Alaghôn circa 270 DR.

History
Alesam Mischwin came to power at the same time as Laviar Illistine, ruler of Hlondeth, began amassing troops near Turmish's borders. The nation was locked in political conflicts between the rich and powerful of the nation – merchant houses. Seeing the looming danger, the noble merchant houses united behind Alesam Mischwin. Hlondeth's troops met equal military force from the Turmishian side. As the battle raged, it became obvious that neither side could get the upper hand, resulting in the conflict being remembered as The Stalemate. Eventually, the Stalemate ended with Chondathan forces retreating due to the loss of the nation's woodcutting operation.

Appendix
References

---

## Alosaka

Alosaka, who was also called the Germinator, was a great spirit of the folklore and myth of the Azuposi people of the Pasocada Basin of northern Maztica.

History
The Germinator was called into being by the song of Sus'sistinako, the Spider Woman. As a spirit of creation, Alosaka helped make all of the flowers, animals, and other life forms found within the Pasocada Basin.

Appendix
References

Connections

---

## Alban Onire

Alban Onire, the Holy Knight of Duty, was a legendary warrior loyal to Torm.

Description
The knight was a handsome man who wore shining plate mail similar to that of his deity, the Loyal Fury. His armor straps and belt smelled of fresh oil, and he owned a gem-covered scabbard. Apparently, he possessed the magical steel sword known as Titanslayer, and when wielded, he could not be harmed by giants.

History
Life
Alban lived centuries prior to the 14 century DR, where he was known to be the bane of both dragons and giants. In his years, he performed many great deeds.

Legacy
As of the 14 century DR, the stories of Alban's might and heroism were known to many, including Torm himself, as well as his followers, such as Gwydion the Quick.

When Gwydion journeyed to Thar to fight Thrym, he came upon the rest place of Onire. Torm commanded him to take up Onire's burden and use his sword in battle. As Gwydion did this, the body of the warrior disappeared. However, when Gwydion used the sword, the powers of Titanslayer did not hold, as if they never did. The warrior was slain by Thrym.

Upon being sent back to the Material Plane after death, Gwydion took up Alban's sword once again. Here he used it to attack Dendar the Night Serpent. As such, Alban Onire's blade of legend became the first weapon to ever injure the Night Serpent.

Appendix
Appearances
Novels
Prince of Lies
References

## Alathan

Alathan was a well-known martyred priest of Lathander who was active long before the 14 century DR.

History
Alathan was a High Priest of the Church of Lathander from the land of Tethyr in the Lands of Intrigue. The cleric rose to prominence when he was persecuted for preaching about the Morning Lord in Tethyr sometime around the late 10 century DR. The local  Tethyrian duke did not stand for the new and unwanted faith growing within his domain. He hated new things and wished his commoners not to get any bright ideas. By the mid 14 century DR, Alathan was renowned enough to become an exclamation of shock and disgust by the laypeople and clerics of Lathander. The exclamation was "By the blood of Alathan!" or shortened to "Bitterblood!". Both clerics of the famed adventuring company – the Knights of Myth Drannor, Doust Sulwood and Semoor Wolftooth, were known to evoke the martyred High Priest's blood.

Appendix
Appearances
Novels

Swords of Eveningstar

## Alert allies

Alert allies was a spell used by clerics of Gorm Gulthyn.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to send a short mental message instantaneously to an ally. This spell did not allow for two-way communication, and the only message the caster could transmit was "alert". The recipient had to be both alive and awake, and a sentient being. They also had to be within  of the caster.

The spell could be cast on several individuals, and lasted eight hours or until the alert was transmitted, whichever came first.

Components
This spell required verbal and somatic components. The somatic component of the spell was the caster physical contact with the recipient during casting.

Appendix
References

---

## Alleena Moss

Alleena Moss was a young girl that lived with her family on a farm in the Twilight Marsh of the Moonsea North during the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
Aleena was the daughter of Millivent and Haldred Moss. She had four brothers and sisters, Bo, Kithian, Quayle, and Volland.

History
Some time during the 1480s DR, the family's farm was raided by goblins from the Foulwater tribe. Alleena and her siblings along with their father were not able to escape the attack in time and appeared to be captured by the goblin raiders. Only Alleena's mother Millivent, and the infant Bo, managed to get away and seek aid.

## Aldo Karnveller

Aldo Karnveller was a militia soldier and a rancher at the Happy Horse Ranch in Triboar in the Dessarin Valley in the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
He was the adult son of Janele Karnveller, owner of the ranch, and brother of Hingo and Rasko.

## Aldenar Uldinath

Aldenar Uldinath was a human swordsmith of the town of Triboar in the Dessarin Valley in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. He was the owner of the Uldinath's Arms smithy.

Activities
Although a swordsmith by trade, Aldenar produced an impressive range of high-quality smithwork of all kinds that were sold in shops found all over the North and along the Sword Coast. His eyebolts, latches, nails, and pins supported Wainwright's Wagons and all Triboar's wainwrights in their success. Though the prices were slightly cheaper than normal, the metal and workmanship were to a higher standard.

Relationships
He maintained a friendly rivalry with fellow smith Ghelryn Foehammer, owner of Foehammer's Forge, which was situated across from the road from Uldinath's Arms. This carried on to his great-granddaughter, Harriet Uldinath.

Appendix
References

## Alias Versus the Night Masks

Alias Versus the Night Masks was a book that recounted the conflict between Alias of the Azure Bonds and the notorious Night Masks of Westgate of 1368 DR, penned by Harper agent Jamal the Thespian.Alias Versus the Night Masks was the in-universe version of the novel Masquerades, penned by Jeff Grubb and Kate Novak.

Content
The book recorded the events of the, when hero Alias and her "brother," saurial paladin Dragonbait visited Westgate and became entangled in plots of the city's criminal underbelly. The duo reunited with their former companion hin "bard" Olive Ruskettle, Mintassan the Sage, and the book's author, Jamal the Thespian. Together, they ended the reign of the mysterious leader of the Night Masks, only known as the Faceless (in reality Victor Dhostar).

The book's foreword praised the heroes, proclaiming the love for Alias as the city's hero, but conceded that true evil could not be easily defeated when it has taken root. Jamal the Thespian stated that by the, a new Faceless rose to power, and the guild was resurgent once again. The author hopes that the book would inspire a new generation of heroes to follow in Alias' footsteps and root the Night Masks' evil out from Westgate's undercut - the Undergate.

History
The book was written and published in 1370 DR, and its copies were available for sale at Mintassan's Mysteries, a store of curios acquired from across the planes by Mintassan the Magnificent.

Appendix
Notes

Appearances
Video Games
Neverwinter Nights 2: Mysteries of Westgate

## Almer Galarthund

{{quote|Tis a lonely thing, to be Magister. All shadows have eyes. Death is never far off, and often charges at one. 'Tis not an office for the faint of heart.*|Thoughts Before My Slaying, by Almer Galarthund}}Almer Galarthund*' was the Magister from  to .

Description
He was tall and lean.

Personality
He was cynical and bitter. He enjoyed watching dances, and listening to melancholic tunes.

Abilities
To become the Magister, Almer had prepared a trap where he rigged a number of wands to shoot on their own.

History
Almer Galarthund prepared a trap for Quaervaxthanus in which he rigged a number of wands to discharge all of their power against the beholder from all directions; he himself was gravely injured in the process, as the wands blew up the mountainside. He found healing, but the memory of that pain made him overly cautious, traveling always in disguise, assiduously employing defensive magic, and striking first at any wizard who got too close. He resolved eventually to train a successor, but was ambushed and slain before he could.

## Alian

Alian was a dwarf innkeep inhabiting the city of Daggerford in the Western Heartlands during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Alian was the owner of the Dragonback Inn. The inn catered mostly to Daggerford's farmers, who spent months at a time tending their crops. The Dragonback Inn was located in the northwest corner of Daggerford along Wall Street, Fields Lane, and Horse Way.

History
Alian enjoyed the life of an adventurer before retiring and buying the Dragonback Inn.

Relationships
Alian lived on the second floor of the Dragonback Inn with his girlfriend and a pet dire wolf hybrid named Sheeba.

## Ale Street

Ale Street was a small road located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the mid–14 century DR.

Location
Ale Street was located in Zhentil Keep's Common Quarter in the eastern section of the city. The road connected Beholder Street with the Street of Sorrow. Black Street intersected Ale Street near its midpoint.

## Alaundo's Loop

Alaundo's Loop was a mysterious magical item apparently associated with the great prophet Alaundo the Seer.

Description
It took the form of a golden crystal shaped like a twisted curl that was constantly turning in on itself.

History
By 1374 DR, Alaundo's Loop was in the possession of Khelben Arunsun and kept within Blackstaff Tower, where it rested on a white velvet cushion on display between the ground and second floors.

Powers
Deep inside, it was said to conceal eternity itself.

## Alley Way

Alley Way was a street located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the mid–14 century DR.

Location
Alley Way was located in southeast Zhentil Keep in the Common Quarter. The road branched south then southwest from the midpoint of Hasbeen Road to terminate along Vice Street and Border Street near the southern wall of the city.

Alair Nalabukhar was the fabled owner of an orb of power in the texts studied by Phelandern Ghemelt.

Rumors & Legends
According to the legends, Alair Nalabukhar, one young son of the extinct Nalabukharn family, had merged himself with an orb of power after his body had grown too mangled to live. He was somewhere in Faerûn.

Albaerum of Neverwinter was the author of The Wonders of Magery, a book that, amongst other things, touched on the mission of the Magister.

History
Circa, Albaerum published four volumes in the series Of the Wonders of Magery.

## Alexander Winter

Alexander Winter was a contract designer for Dungeons & Dragons. He is no longer in the roleplaying gaming design industry, and after working as a student researcher, is now a software engineer.https://www.linkedin.com/in/alexandercwinter/details/experience/

Works
Alexander was the designer for adventures such as Murder in Baldur's Gate and the The Rise of Tiamat. In addition, he wrote an adventure for Dungeon magazine 217.

## Alliya Macanester

Honored Mother Alliya Macanester, also known as the Old Lady of Corm Orp, was a halfling priestess of Sheela Peryroyl and the Matriarch of the clergy at the Ladyhouse, a temple in the Sunset Vale near Corm Orp. She was the ruler of Corm Orp in all but name as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Alliya was especially wise, and was extraordinarily insightful in all topics agricultural. She had an unrivaled familiarity and knowledge of the nature and climate of the Sunset Vale. She was said to be able to predict the weather, to tell if a seed would grow simply by looking at it, and to be able to recommend the exact best place, time, and manner in which to plant crops. Some even claimed that she could revive withered plants with just a touch.

She was known for her diligence and wisdom as a leader. She could commanded obedience, and was known to be fierce in the face of her enemies, in some cases willing to violate the tenants of her goddess to deal with threats.

Relationships
She was much loved and venerated among the halflings of Corm Orp, and as a result, she was the de facto ruler of the village, and the actual lord obeyed her commands. Likewise, her temple became the de facto center of the village's religious, agricultural, and social activities.

She was a sworn enemy of the Zhentarim and their designs in the Sunset Vale. In the course of trying to defeat their plans, she had resorted to various violent acts, including using poison and hiring adventurers to deal with them.

History
Alliya was originally from Evenstar Vale. She earned the trust of the farmers and folk of Corm Orp after she saved the local crops by accurately predicting both a drought in the, and the "Great Frost" in the . She also made sure that Corm Orp was prepared for attacks from starving monsters which accompanied both of these famines.

By the mid-to-late 14 century DR, she was so beloved and influential among the local halfings around Corm Orp that the village's ruler, Lord Dundast Hulrel, obeyed her completely. Around this same time, some halflings began discussing the formation of a halfling kingdom in the region, which was to be called Sheeland and would be ruled by Alliya as its first queen. Alliya herself would dismiss such suggestions with a laugh and a reminder that petty kingdoms always ended tragically, but it was rumored that she had considered wielding more direct power if the threat from the Zhentarim continued to grow.

Appendix
References

## Alnether Street

Alnether Street was a road that was located in the city of Waterdeep.

Location
Alnether Street was located in the northern section of Waterdeep's Castle Ward. The road orignated on Bazaar Street, branching south where it ran parallel to the Street of Silver to the west and Warrior's Way to the east. Alnether Street merged with the western end of Cymbril's Walk near the Street of Silver's intersection with Keltarn Street. The Prowl branched off the southeast end of Alnether Street.

Appendix
References

## Allanna

Allanna was a river dweller tam'hi sorceress from the jungles of the Malatran Plateau active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Allanna was known for her peaceful nature and ability to be a successful peacemaker.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Tribe of the Wise Ones became aware of a looming threat. The leader of the dreaded spider katanga, the Mother of a Thousand Young was swollen with a thousand children ready to overflow the jungles. In preparation for the birthing, she initiated a ritual that sent deadly poisons into the rivers cascading off the Fire Mountain. Allanna was among the younger and less experienced heroes of the Living Jungle who answered the call. Allanna's group was sent to enter the spider katanga caverns and faced hunting spider katangas who guarded previously captured adventurers.

It was unknown if Allanna survived the battle, but the Mother of a Thousand Young was defeated, and heroes returned to the Village of the Wise Ones for their reward and facilitated the clean-up and nature's healing.

Appendix
Appearances

## Alrina Skyeyes

Alrina Skyeyes was a wizard sailing aboard the Osprey during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
She stood  tall. She had soft brown eyes and many men found her attractive.

Activities
Alrina served as ship's wizard aboard the Osprey. Captain Raider McAllister rescued her father when she was much younger, and the two were friends for many years. She loved life at sea but often thought of adventuring on the land in order to acquire more magic items.

Possessions
Alrina carried a cloak of protection, wand of lightning bolts, decanter of endless water, ring of water elemental control, and several scrolls and potions.

Skills & Abilities
She could cast the following spells—comprehend languages, enlarge, magic missile, mending, read magic, sleep, ESP, fog cloud, stinking cloud, web, gust of wind, lightning bolt, protection from normal missiles, rainbow pattern, Evard's black tentacles, and ice storm''.

Appendix
References

---

## Alsapir's Rock

Alsapir's Rock was a tiny island in the Red Rocks in the Sea of Swords of the Sword Coast North. Notably, it was once the lair of the copper dragon Gaulauntyr, called Glorytongue.

Geography
Part of the Red Rocks archipelago, it lay just north of Waterdeep and so near the coast and Mount Sar that it was rarely marked on maps and sea charts.

Notable Locations
On its western side was a many-branched sea cave carved out by the waves, with tidal waters flooding its entrance. Here was where Gaulauntyr had made her lair. Although she had moved away by 1372 DR, she left behind her food source—a captive deepspawn that produced boars, deer, rothé, and sundry other prey animals to satisfy her appetite. Unfortunately, while the deepspawn kept birthing them, there was no food for them and no way out of the caves, so they simply starved and died.

History
It acquired its name after a fisherman called Alsapir met his death here when a storm smashed his boat against the rock.

Appendix
References

## Alsapir

Alsapir was a fisherman of the Sword Coast North.

He was fishing in the Sea of Swords around the Red Rocks north of Waterdeep when a storm smashed his boat against one of the rocky islets, and he died there. That island came to be called Alsapir's Rock by the 1370s DR.

## Alter digits

Alter digits was a spell.

Effects
For a minute or more, the caster could alter the target's fingers and toes in size, hue, shape, texture, or any combination thereof. They could also be made to exude sweat that was sticky or slippery. Number of digits would not be changed by this spell.

Components
The spell called only for somatic and verbal components.

History
Azuth made this spell available through Elminster sometime prior to

## Alakin

Alakin was the precept of the town of Aina in Mulhorand during the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
Alakin was a priest of Thoth. His protection extended to the rich farmland surrounding the mouth of the River of Swords.

References

## Allergy field

Allergy field was an uncommon spell for rangers and rarely used by druids.

Effects
When cast, this spell triggered plants to produce allergens and antigens such as pollen. It affected a minimum area of  per side, and this area increased as the caster grew more skilled. It was often cast in areas with abundant plant life such as forests and meadows. Any creature within the area of effect experienced severe allergies, including sneezing, headaches and swelling around the eyes. The caster determined the duration of the spell, but the first frost would break the spell.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component a small amount of ragweed.

Appendix
References

## Aldon Bargewright

Aldon Bargewright was a human ranger inhabiting the settlement of Bargewright Inn within the Dessarin Valley in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Relationships
Aldon's brother, Feston Bargewright, was the innkeeper of The Bargewright Inn and founder and leader of the settlement.

Activities
Aldon led the militia of Bargewright Inn by 1358 DR and through the mid-1360s DR. These comprised five trained fighters (hostelers at the inn) and a score of shopkeepers all wielding pikes. He also served as the stablemaster at the inn. By 1370 DR, he had also succeeded his brother as chairman of the Council of Directors that managed the settlement.

Possessions
He carried a +1 longsword, rumored by others to be enchanted.

Appendix
References

---

## Alvagaerus Firlthond

Alvagaerus Firlthond was a mage of the Zhentarim in the late 13 century DR.

History
He was taught how to create blast-globes by Isilger Mountrant during the latter's tenure as Magister, between  and . Later, he became an apprentice of Manshoon during the initial founding of the Zhentarim; his chief rival Sememmon mind-reamed the secret of the blast-globes out of him, and slew him. These secrets were traded for the latter's initial rise in rank, which happened in .

Appendix
References

## Alter beast

Alter beast was a powerful arcane alteration spell that permanently changed a physical aspect of a creature.

Effects
A creature's anatomy could be modified with this spell through an incredibly painful and detailed process. The subject was unconscious and restrained for the casting. Any changes made with alter beast could be passed to the creature's offspring.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component two strands of gold wire twisted together to cast. A full wizard's laboratoy was also needed for all of the spell's preparations.

History
Hssthak, a sarrukh mummy, carried a Nether Scroll with the alter beast spell.

Appendix
References

---

## Alter reality

Alter reality was an arcane illusion spell that could temporarily make changes in reality that suited the caster.

Effects
The caster envisioned the change they wanted to happen during the spell's casting. They did this by speaking it aloud and casting a phantasmal force version of the change. Typically, only minor changes could be affected without dire consequences. Alter reality was a double-edged sword, with caster's trying to change too much often seeing the spell backfire upon them.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component part of the caster's life force as casting this spell aged the caster slightly.

History
The alter reality spell could be used to destroy the rod of mirrors.

Appendix
Appearances

References

## Alandor eels

Alandor eels was a dish made using the animals of the same name that were abundant in the Alandor River in Zehoarastria region of Amn.

Description
Alandor eels were big black eels found in the Alandor River, with the brownscale fish being their main prey. Alandor eels were fished using worm ball bait by the locals off of bridges and docks and prepared into a dish of questionable taste. Alandor eels were cooked in handfuls of pepper and drowned in lemon juice.

History
In his guide to the Lands of Intrigue, published in the late 14 century DR, famous (or infamous) writer Volothamp Geddarm described the honor of eating Alandor eels. He thought the creatures to be disgusting and the dish to be rubbery and tasting like dead fish despite all the peppercorns and lemon acid.

Appendix
References

---

## Alavar

Alavar was a harpist of some renown, active sometime before 1372 DR.

History
At some point before the late 14 century DR, Alavar was known for owning the Instrument of the Winds, a unique enchanted musical instrument of sinister power. The magic harp captured souls of its past players, making the sounds produced by it especially haunting. At some point, the Instrument of the Winds captured the souls of both Alavar's parents, and he spent the rest of his life using the instrument and searching for a way to free the souls trapped within. It was unknown of Alavar's fate, but the Instrument of the Winds was still in existence, playing its haunting melodies as of the .

## Al-Nasr

Al-Nasr, which was Midani for the Vulture, was a mysterious ajami necromancer who inhabited the city of Hilm in the Pantheist League of interior Zakhara during the mid–14 century DR.

Relationships
Little was known about Al-Nasr save that he tutored both Ezarhaddon bin Kior and his wife Inanna az-Khunjhati, both of whom would later become the Necromancer King and Queen of the ruined city of Ysawis, in the dark arts of necromancy.

History
Althidon had perished about two centuries prior to the, roughly in the year of the . The casements of Alastrarra were Althidon's work, and the finest in Cormanthor as of 241 DR.

## Alaglossa Tornglara

Lady Akaglossa Tornglara was an inhabitant of Cormanthor in the 3 century DR. She was involved in the raising of the Mythal.

History
In, during a party at Satyrdance Pool, she and a number of other ladies shared concerns about the future of Cormanthor; in particular, she believed it might be destroyed within her lifetime. They then agreed to try and nudge their husbands towards stabilizing the city. After the hunt for armathor Elminster Aumar, she was discussing their successes in the matter, when they were stopped by Elandorr Waelvor; he implied strongly that he would harm them if they got in his way.

In, during the laying of the Mythal, Alaglossa Tornglara was briefly frightened by the negative reaction of her servant, Nlaea, who appeared to come to harm by the Mythal'''s power.  However, she ultimately was a contributor to the energy that the Mythal required.

Rumors
Alaglossa Tornglara was said to scry distant cities to discover new fashions.

Appendix
Appearances
NovelsElminster in Myth Drannor''

## Altar of Scales

The Altar of Scales was a temple dedicated to Tiamat, the Dragon Queen within the Untheric pantheon.

Location
The temple lay within the catacombs beneath the ruins of Unthalass in Unther.

History
The Altar of Scales survived the destruction of Unthalass when Tiamat and Gilgeam battled through the city, flourishing in the anarchy that ensured with the city's destruction. The leader of the temple at this time was High Priestess Shudu-Ab.

Appendix
References

## Alathallgo Gathalynker

Lord Alathallgo Gathalynker was a member of the wealthy but no noble Gathalynker family of Amn, famously killed circa 1270 DR by his orc slaves.

Description
Alathallgo was an obese man and a cruel slave driver. The wore an enchanted set of armor when traveling along with his humanoid livestock. His fingers were decorated with ensorcelled rings and several magic blades, wands, and daggers hung on his waist.

History
Alathallgo Gathalynker was believed to hadbeen slain by a group of orcish shaves who rebelled and attacked their master while traveling the Orc Road in Zehoarastria sometime in the late 13 century DR. His body was thought to be hidden and buried somewhere along the road that stretched between the settlements of Crimmor and Purskul. His body was still adorned in the Lord's magic armor. Alathallgo's skull was uncovered in the, still wearing his distinct decorated helmet.

The slaver's torso nor his magic armor were found as of the late 14 century DR. The Gathalynker family regularly hired workers to embark on excavation expeditions in search of the family's famous ancestor's body. Some believed that the orcs consumed the slaver's body after killing him, while others speculated that Alathallgo Gathalynker's body was buried elsewhere. There was also a chance that the body was discovered many years prior; all his magic loot – sold off.

Appendix
References

---

## Alandamer's Gate

Alandamer's Gate was the northern gate of the city of Athkatla in Amn.

Location
Alandamer's Gate stood on the border of two districts of Athkatla: the Wave District to the west and the Temple District to the east. The road that passed Alandamer's Gate ran north towards the grand temple complex of the Goldspires and the monastery Arbalest's House.

Activities
All non-citizens who passed Alandamer's Gate had to pay an "any one coin" toll, of gold or silver, to be allowed into Athkatla.

## Albaerlus Gudelmer

Albaerlus Gudelmer was the patriarch of the wealthy merchant Gudelmer family of Athkatla in the late 14 century DR.

Description
After the death of Albaerlus's wife, his business tactics became riskier, if not reckless, than before. He cared not for blackmail attempts, and his opponents always failed at attempts to seduce the man.

Relationships
Albaerlus had four affable and beautiful daughters: Raeveshara was the oldest of the Gudelmer sisters, followed by Iylandra and the youngest twin siblings – Quaerthara and Nilunele.

Possessions
By the late 14 century DR, Albaerlus Gudelmer and his daughters resided in the family mansion, famously haunted and beautiful Dancing Dolphins House in Athkatla's resplendent Gem District. Like many merchants of Amn, Albaerlus owned properties throughout Athkatla.

Activities
Albaerlus Gudelmer was an ambitious and influential merchant who built the family fortune by dealing in sails and imported glassware. He sought to increase his sway in Athkatla through the formation of alliance circles he referred to as "shieldrings." Each of the shieldrings was formed of a group of merchants and business people of lower social stature, banded together to effectively stand against the more wealthy and influential merchant families of Amn, such as Argrims, Copriths, and Vymmars. Membership was cemented with a written contract and the fee of 6 danters for inhabitants of Athkatla, 12 danters for those outside of the city, and up to 20 roldons for more exclusive shieldrings.

To show the lack of ill intent, Albaerlus Gudelmer regularly threw lavish parties in the Dancing Dolphins House for independent merchants from the city and wider Amn. During these events, Albaerlus actively promoted his views of "a better, happier Amn for all." Merchants and traders from outside of Amn were always welcomed, expanding the shieldrings.

As of the late 14 century DR, these existed ten shieldrings, represented by ten colorfully-decorated shields that were displayed in the Dancing Dolphins House. They were: Brightbar (red warhorn), Waterdeep (blue snail shell), Boldlance (red spear), Allcircle (eight golden coins), Fairseason (crossed gold and green leaves), Manymoons (sun and moon), Windcoast (three cogs), Glowcrown (three sapphires), Pearlbright (ring of five pearls), and Shiningbeast (crossed unicorn horns).

History
By the late 14 century DR, Albaerlus Gudelmer managed to avoid direct conflicts with guilds, merchant families, and the Council of Five; however, his family and dealings faced some scrutiny and several investigations into the man's motives. The Council informants were always present during Gudelmer family feasts and parties thrown in the family's manse. At one point, the Council of Five set a trap for Albaerlus's operations. The council's agents tried to have the merchant involve one of his shieldrings – "Pearlbright," in a kidnapping and slave sale plot. Despite being promised great profits, Gudelmer turned it down.

Through his activities, Albaerlus managed to negotiate lower prices on various goods purchased in bulk by Athkatlan shops, allowing lower-priced goods to be available to the wider population of the City of Coin.

As of the late 14 century DR, the notorious criminal power – the Shadow Thieves, left Albaerlus' activities unmolested, possibly allowing the merchant network to grow only to be tapped into at some later date.

## Alaghar

Alaghor were specialty priests devoted to Clangeddin Silverbeard, the Father of Battle.

Culture
The vast majority of alaghor were either gold dwarves or shield dwarves, but members of all of the dwarf races, both males and females, could become an alaghar.

Abilities
Alaghar possessed superior skills with weapons, similar to warriors. They practiced their weapons skills frequently, allowing them to strike more often during combat. An alaghar could pour all of their energy into a devastating swing of their weapon once per day.

In addition to their normal spells, alaghar could cast command, spiritual hammer, strength, strength of one, defensive harmony, axe storm of Clangeddin, and blade barrier on a daily basis. They could also detect magic at will.

Possessions
Alaghor did not have restrictions on the type of weapon they were permitted to wield nor the armor they could wear. Many favored the battle axe.
