## Alana Hallwinter

Alana Hallwinter was a resident of Phandalin in the late 15 century DR. She was the adopted daughter of Sildar Hallwinter.

Description
She was a capable miner who wore ribbons in her hair.

Personality
Alana was a smart but foolhardy girl. She was said to be obsessed with stories of fine ladies and was a fan of pretty things like ribbons and flowers.

Relationships
She was close friends with a trio of dwarves and fellow teenagers named Nedd Angelite, Sasha Flint, and Grace Hardstone.

Her father, Sildar, was very protective of her and did not trust her to track or travel alone in the woods. Even so, he permitted her to travel unsupervised as far afield from Phandalin as Swallow Lake to swim with her friends.

History
When she was a teenager in the 1490s DR, she became concerned when her three dwarven friends—Nedd, Sasha, and Grace—disappeared into the Crooked Forest while seeking proof of some of the things they had read about in the newly-published Volo's Guide to Monsters. She decided to go look for them, but did not tell her father. Instead, she told him that she was meeting with her friends to go swimming, and left a note explaining her true intentions for him to find later. When he discovered it, he feared that the teens' disappearance could start a panic, and so he concealed the information from the townsfolk and from his fellow members of the Town Council of Phandalin. Feeling like he was unable to go after her without alerting the town to the disappearances, he instead sought out adventurers and offered to reward them not only for finding his daughter, but for their discretion.

Meanwhile, Alana had met with the same fate as her friends in the Crooked Forest: she was captured by gnolls, who blindfolded her and took her to their camp. Although bleeding and terrified, Alana managed to remove her blindfold and sneakily leave a trail for any rescuers to follow by tying ten of her ribbons onto branches as her captors carried her deeper into the forest and higher into the trees. During this episode, she caught a glimpse of Gavmogon, although did not realize what it was.

## Aleka Ravenheart

Aleka Ravenheart was an officer of the Purple Dragons in charge of the garrison of Thistleflame Keep in Suzail, who served in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Aleka had an athletic build and wore her black hair in a long braid.

Personality
She was dependable, dutiful, and took her position very seriously, though she could be lured into making foolhardy decisions.

Possessions
She wore chainmail armor and carried a longsword, in addition to a ring of regeneration.

Relationships
Aleka was good friends with Haverish Hallefon, innkeep of the Bubbling Bulwark and a former soldier in her regiment.

History
In 1369 DR, she was in charge of the 40-strong Purple Dragons garrison of Thistleflame Keep in Suzail. When it appeared that Lord Partic Thistle had been mysteriously murdered, she secured the premises and deputized a group of adventurers to help find the perpetrators. She unintentionally provided the bard Mendryll Belarod with an alibi by spending the night with him, though she denied all involvement with him to save her professional reputation.

Her investigation ultimately uncovered the involvement of the Emerald Swords adventuring company, who had actually kidnapped Lord Thistle and murdered the doppelganger Xixis in his place. As it was "merely' kidnapping and not the murder of a noble, she had the authority to have the Swords exiled from Cormyr, rather than executed.

## Alanis Flamehair

Description
Like many Sunite priests, she had a beautiful appearance and red hair.

Personality
Alanis was extremely vain, and abhorred receiving any kind of wound to her body. She was also squeamish about violence in general.

Abilities
Alanis used her clerical powers to support her team of adventurers, preferring spells that kept them alive rather than harming their enemies.

Possessions
In combat, she wore chain mail and wielded a club. In addition to her holy symbol and a few vials of holy water, she carried a staff of curing and a ring of regeneration.

History
In the, she and the other Stag Runners attended a party at Thistleflame Keep. The entertainer Mendryll Belarod proceeded to perform a shockingly debauched and insulting song that featured two Sembite merchants, a dryad, and a harpy, and the Stag Runners collectively decided to get revenge on him. Later that night, they attacked Mendryll, but between Mendryll's own skills and another group of adventurers, the Stag Runners were defeated. That same night they were also implicated in a murder by the Emerald Swords adventuring company, but may have been exonerated by virtue of being arrested at the time.

## Alanis Dirgesong

Alanis Dirgesong was a traveling bard who specialized in depressing and despondent songs in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Alanis had long black hair and gray eyes. She dressed in a white blouse with a black leather bodice and leggings.

Personality
She had a constantly gloomy mood, and enjoyed wallowing in self-pity and woe in any circumstance.

Abilities
Alanis had a variety of bardic magical abilities that she used to protect herself and enhance her performances. She adored portraying her spells as evidence of her futile struggle against her doomed fate.

Possessions
Alanis carried a short sword and wore enchanted +5 leather armor. Her tool of the trade was a black harp, and she also carried a ring of free action and an onyx dog figurine. She traveled about on a pony.

Activities
Alanis traveled the Realms and performed her music widely, gaining a significant following despite her depressing and pitiful persona. She wrote and performed songs about subjects such as doomed lovers, infants dying in the crib, futile struggles against fate, and adventurers that were lost and forgotten. He work was adored by Lord Rhyn Mournsoul in particular.

History
In the, she was traveling in eastern Cormyr and stayed overnight at the Journey's Rest inn. When the vampire innkeepers Ryanna and Moria attacked the other customers, she immediately accepted her death and began composing an epitaph. Fortunately for Alanis, the vampires did not consider her a target as she was too famous and her disappearance would draw too much attention to the inn.

Appendix
Trivia
Alanis Dirgesong is likely a reference to the real-life singer and songwriter Alanis Morissette, who is not quite as depressing.

## Altho Minstrelwish

Altho Minstrelwish was a world-hopping halfling explorer and trader of the Minstrelwish family in the 1 century DR.

History
Around, Altho traveled to the world of Earth, most likely to the realm of France, and collected a number of foxes. He imported these foxes to Toril, where he had plans to sell their fur (particularly their tails, termed "brushes") for fashionable attire, but found few halflings were interested because of their distinct smell. Fox meat, whether roasted or stewed, was even less popular.

In the end, Altho let the foxes run wild, thereby introducing foxes to Faerûn, and in time they displaced the native Faerûnian lynx. This origin of foxes was remembered in the hin word for "fox", rennard, after the French word for a fox, renard.Foxes have appeared in earlier-set sources, such as in the time of Netheril in the novel Sword Play, suggesting Altho was responsible for a different breed of fox or the reintroduction of foxes.

Appendix
Notes

## All-consuming wasting

All-consuming wasting was a magical disease that transformed the afflicted into an all-consuming hunger.

Acquisition
The disease was primarily inflicted when an all-consuming hunger swarmed a victim, however it was possible for other undead creatures to spread the disease as well. If the infection took root, symptoms began to manifest in about one day.

Effects
All-consuming wasting was said to be similar to mummy rot. It ate away at sufferers' stamina and physical constitution as it putrefied flesh and reduced organs to jelly in preparation for transforming the victim into an all-consuming hunger. It could take several days for the disease to kill an infected individual, at which point their rotten body parts would animate as a new all-consuming hunger less than half a minute later.

Treatment
While a strong immune system—and a great deal of luck—could stave off the progression of the disease, only magical healing, such as remove disease, could allow an infected individual to recover from all-consuming wasting.

History
Sometime prior to the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the Keeper of Thaal devised a means to create unique undead abominations that could spread all-consuming wasting. The only known success was achieved using the corpse of a bard named Askilion.

Appendix
Appearances

## Aluianti

Aluianti was a village of gold elves in Semberholme.

Description
Aluianti was one of the smaller settlements within the region, typically housing no more than fifty residents at any given time. The village boasted an exclusive populace of gold elves, although it remained open and welcoming to all elves.

Trade
At the heart of Aluianti's cultural identity lied its artisans, whose creative endeavors garnered widespread admiration. The inhabitants of the village were primarily skilled artisans, channeling their expertise into crafting exquisite pottery, exceptional paintings, and masterful jewelry adorned with precious gems. Aluianti's artistic offerings have earned it a reputation for producing works of unparalleled beauty and craftsmanship.

History
Aluianti was established in The Year of Tomes, -54 DR. Seeking respite from the tumultuous political climate in Cormanthor and driven by concerns for the clan's prosperity, the influential heads of the Aluianti families made the pivotal decision to relocate to this idyllic locale.

## Alley Plague

The Alley Plague was a disease outbreak that struck the Shoon Imperium in the .

Acquisition
The plague was spread through the air, especially in the vicinity of corpses of its victims.

Effects
The disease entailed pox and impaired the blood's ability to clot.

History
The Alley Plague ravaged Almraiven and Calimport as well as the docks of Manshaka. In most cities, casualties climbed to 17%, while in Calimport, casualties rose to 21%, owing to the accumulation of rotting corpses in the streets of Upper Calimport.

Appendix
References

## Alfin Biscop

Alfin Biscop was a wealthy cleric of Torm and an adventurer who had retired to Neverwinter as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
Alfin came under the effects of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, which caused his body to wither and rot: his hair fell out, his skin sagged, and his nose decayed away. However, it did not impact his sharp mind or his deep voice.

Personality
He was famous for his love of chess.

He valued self-discipline. He had no mercy or sympathy for thieves or evildoers unless he had a use for them, and he had no issue with keeping "evil" creatures imprisoned or enslaved if doing so could further the greater good.

When he became stricken with the death curse, he began to question his choices and his worldview, wondering whether he might be being punished for his actions or for his wealthy lifestyle.

Abilities
He was a powerful healer, able to slow the course of poisons that were supposed to be instantly fatal.

Possessions
He had a mansion in Neverwinter, the floors of which were decorated in a checker pattern to look like a lanceboard. He displayed many trophies from his adventuring days, including a gorgon which had been fashioned into a table and an iron golem which had been fashioned into a magical door; a stuffed owlbear and girallon; an orrery; and a number of weapons, including a barbazu's glaive, an erinyes's rope, and an osyluth's hooked polearm.

The trap which protected his vault was armed with an incurable poison from the tail of Geryon himself, and consisted of many mephits and imps trapped within glass chess pieces.

He owned a special telescope fashioned from living, malfunctioning modrons which he used to study the cosmos.

In battle, he had wielded an adamantine mace.

Activities
Alfin was a minor celebrity in Neverwinter such that his presence was looked for and expected during holidays and festivals. He was also believed to occasionally set out to travel Faerûn on his own.

Relationships
Alfin was on good terms with the Harpers, and tended to trust them. He also had a number of trusted servants, some of whom he would send out on missions of great importance. He maintained limited contact with his old adventuring companions.

He also had many enemies who would gladly strike him down in the streets if they could.

History
In his youth, Alfin had campaigned in the name of Torm. He had faced many powerful foes and notably many devils. He was said to have carried a chessboard on all of his expeditions, and even to have gambled his life in games of chess against a dragon on two separate occasions: once against a silver dragon in which he won, and once against a blue dragon in which he lost and had to be revived by his temple. He was one of several members of his adventuring group who had died and been resurrected.

When he retired to his mansion in Neverwinter, he commissioned the warlock Rokh Manti to create both a devious chess-based trap to protect his vault as well as his telescope for studying the planes.

When the death curse began, Alfin withdrew from the public eye as he tried to find out the cause of it. He sent his servants far and wide to learn of the curse and to determine the fate of his old adventuring companions, and used his modron telescope to study the planes. Eventually, as he lost hope as his body decayed, and dismissed his remaining servants.

After six months, his absence led many to start to worry about where he had gone, and also led the Shadow Thieves of Neverwinter to assume that his mansion was unattended. After having learned of his love of chess from Rokh Manti, they correctly surmised that his vault would be locked in some way involving the game. Thus, they had one of their agents, Pion, sharpen his chess skills in preparation for burgling the mansion. This went poorly, with Pion fatally poisoned. However, Alfin slowed the poison in his system, and blackmailed the thief into delivering a message to the Harpers: he believed that he had finally discovered that the death curse had something to do with the flow of positive energy to Toril from the Positive Energy plane.

## Alkaline salt

Alkaline salt or alkali was a corrosive mineral salt used in a variety of trades.

Availability
 Alkaline salts were refined from natural water sources and arid soils.
 Alkaline lakes in Zakhara's deserts such as the Pit of the Ghuls were a source of alkaline salts, bromine salts, and table salt, but were often too remote and dangerous to exploit.
 Aurora's Emporium sold alkaline salt for 1 sp per .

Usages
 Alkali was used to neutralize strong vitriols, though was itself corrosive and dangerous when concentrated.
 Many alchemical recipes used alkali.
 Alkali was used to make strong red dyes with cinnabar.
 Certain medicines used alkali, including a treatment for mad monkey fever.
 Alkali was an ingredient in making a strong putty, along with refined pitch.
 Enamels were sometimes produced using alkali.
 The spell airy water could use a small handful of alkaline salts as a material component.

## Alter Egos

Alter Egos is a one-round tournament adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in 1998, and intended for adventurers of all levels.

Summary
The Merchant District of Ravens Bluff became a hotbed of thievery and illegal protection racket in 1371 DR. Surprisingly, several well-known heroes of the Living City were the culprits. One group of heroes that were fingered for the crimes was away on a diplomatic mission to Kurth. The Merchant Watch's believed that the heroes magically transported themselves to and from the Living City to use their mission to Kurth as an alibi. However, Judge Elias "Deathstroke" Mulgany knew better and intercepted the heroes as they returned to Ravens Bluff. He tasked them with investigation and clearing their name but warned them that the Merchant Watch would not be as kind if the adventurers were to be arrested. The group interrogated store owners who claimed that they were the racketeers. Bouquet Boutique, Ephram's Antiquities, Madame LaTachia's, Malishar's Magicks, The Baker Boys, and Wycke's were the names of the shops. Eventually, the adventurers followed the evidence to a group of scoundrels who were part of Myrkyssa Jelan's army during the war. They used a wand of alter self to extort, steal, and collect payments from across the district. After a confrontation, it was believed that the criminals were apprehended by the same adventurers they impersonated. However, some accords claimed that the criminals escaped justice in a commotion of a collapsing ruined temple of Cyric.

## Allamon Darahanis

Allamon Darahanis was a Cyricist cleric and a wizard member of the Zhentarim who operated in the town of Dragon Falls and in the the Vast region in the late 14 century DR.

Abilities
As a lowly priest of Cyric, Allamon had access to several simple spells of the divine nature, such as: cure minor wounds, detect magic, bane, protection from good, and shield of faith. However, as a mage, he was proficient in arcane spells from up to sixth circle of magic. Some of the more notable spells in his repertoire included Otiluke's freezing sphere, teleport, polymorph other, Evard's black tentacles, haste, dispel magic, hold person, and mage armor, among others. He also possessed the knowledge and skills of arcana and spellcraft that allowed him to empower, extend, and quicken spells.

Possessions
Allamon was armed with a simple dagger and wore the unholy symbol of Cyric. He also carried a small enchanted item – a rusty gem, that when activated, summoned a rust monster under the gem-owner's command.

History
On the 17 of Flamerule, the, Allamon Darahanis was tasked by his superior, Threll Whitebane of the Zhentarim, to recover the recently stolen artifact – a Book of Vile Darkness. At 7:28 AM on Flamerule 17, Allamon led a group of Zhents under the effects of the invisibility sphere spell to the home of Harris Redfire on the outskirts of Dragon Falls. Harris was a Mystran who created an inter-dimensional vault for storing evil artifacts securely out of the hands of malicious individuals and servants of dark gods. At 7:49 AM, Allamon used the passwall spell and attacked Redfire in his bedroom. By 7:52 AM, Allamon and his men subdued the Mystran mage, and with the use of the alter self spell, Allamon assumed Redfire's guise.

When a group of adventurers from the Living City arrived carrying the lockbox with the Banite Book of Vile Darkness, they were created by "Harris Redfire," who attempted to fool them into leaving the tome with him. From here, the accounts of the events varied. Some claimed that the heroes entrusted him the precious cargo only to realize their mistake and confront the Zhents in a cave, also rescuing the real Harris Redfire, while others swore that Allamon Darahanis was unable to fool the group, and a spell-battle broke out. One way or another, Allamon was subdued but refused to cooperate with the heroes any further.

## Alaurum

History
Hundreds of years before the late 14 century DR, Alaurum, Ilyndele, and Sharth built the inn itself. The demiplane within which the inn resided—and the plane's many portals—had already existed, supposed by some cosmologists to be a dreamscape resulting from a sleeping fragment of the World Serpent.

## Alexander Rainforest

Alexander Rainforest was an inhabitant of the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Alexander was a male high elf with chizled features, a light bronze skin, and wavy brown hair.

Posessions
Alexander Rainforest owned a house in the Lower City, neighboring Beehive Goods and Wares general store.

Relationships
Ratin Sesescor was the name of a rat what lived in Alexander's home. The man tried to befriend the rat, feeding it various cheeses, earning him the name "cheese-dropper." Alexander discovered that the rat liked red, white, yellow, mellow, and sharp cheeses crumbled into small bits, and disliked smoked, blue, and waxed. When Alexander was killed, Ratin eagerly ate the "red morsels" spilled from his friend's body.

History
Alexander Rainforest was born and bred Baldurian. He worked as an ombudsman in Baldur's Gate's docks. His job led him to investigate the ownership of Candulhallow's Tombstones and the activities of murderous cultists – something that his friends and co-workers thought to be just mad conspiracy theories. Alexander spied on the business and even hired Zhentarim agents. His discoveries were dismissed as ravings, and his friends claimed that this obsession made him dull. These friends were more interested in consuming copious amounts of alcohol at The Singing Lute. The investigations made Alexander Rainforest a target for the serial killer Dolor, who sought to join the unholy Murder Tribunal of Bhaal. He was murdered in the, shortly before the armies of the Cult of the Absolute attacked the city of Baldur's Gate. His body was left in his Lower City home, missing a hand, taken by the killer. Rainforest's tombstone was commissioned to be carved by Candulhallow's Tombstones in the Lower City. The grave marker was stored in Candulhallow's workshop. It read: "Alexander Rainforest. Meticulous in life, mellifluous in death."

## Alioramus

Alioramus and the larger variety known as alioramus colossa were a species of carnivorous dinosaurs (or garuda) found inhabiting the jungles of Chult on the Chultan Peninsula.

Description

Alioramus were dangerous and meticulous hunters. They had powerful jaws that could crush a set of inch-thick plate mail. They had colorful hides and feathers and clawed three-toed limbs.

Combat
Alioramus attacked with their sharp, raking claws and powerful jaws, causing open wounds and bleeding. In combat, they pounced on their victims, pushing them to the ground into a vulnerable position. The largest of the species, alioramus colossa, could provoke a mating frenzy by exuding mating pheromones, driving its pack into a berserker-like rage, strengthening the pack's attacks and making them temporarily resistant to harm and unaffected by charm or fear.

## Alamaqu

Alamaqu was a tent city in the Plain of Horses of northwestern Kara-Tur. It was the home of the T'aghur tribe.

Geography
Situated in the central Chigidi Mountains, the city lay in a valley surrounded by high peaks that formed a defensive barrier. Nearby stood Mount Juqan. A road from Alamaqu ran westward and linked it to the other cities of the Plain of Horses.

Description
Alamaqu was actually little more than a vast campsite containing thousands of tents, collectively as big and as populous as an average city elsewhere. These tents were the distinctive white ger favored by the T'aghurs, arrayed in long lines along paths wide enough ox- and horse-drawn carts. Each was erected over a shallow pit dug into the earth. In the winter, the T'aghurs packed up their tents and drove their sheep herds to greener fields, leaving Alamaqu nearly empty.

Defenses
As well as the mountains themselves, Alamaqu was protected by a stone wall some  high. While the T'aghurs were away in winter, a small garrison remained behind to watch over the campsite.

Circa, a force of 300 elephants was also kept ready for war at Alamaqu. These were the result of the T'aghur triumvirate's decision to adopt elephants as a cavalry, but they proved to be too slow and too willful for Plainsmen warfare. Regardless, the triumvirate obstinately refused to abandon the idea.

History
Around, T'aghur rebels at Alamaqu were planning to depose the tribe's ruling triumvirate, when the traveling lama Torgoja P'a arrived and, in only days, persuaded the rebel leaders to give up their plan.

## Aluwand

Aluwand  was the capital city of Selu'Maraar, a sea elf kingdom beneath the waves of the Dragon Reach.

Geography
In relation to the surface world, Aluwand was located roughly  east of the Scar, somewhere around the Gloom depths of the Inner Sea.The kingdom of Selu'Maraar is stated to reside  beneath the waves of the Dragon Reach. This would put it in the Gloom level of the Inner Sea's ocean depths, which is stated on page 39 of Sea of Fallen Stars to be in the  range.

Government
This city was the seat of power for the ruler of Selu'Maraar, who held the classical Faerûnian elf title of Coronal.

History
Aluwand was constructed around  by refugees of Cormanthyr, who had transformed themselves into sea elves through use of the artifact known as the Sashelan Glass. It later became incorporated into Selu'Maraar, which was founded in  by the Thuvys clan, but would not become its capital until much later.

Following the end of the disastrous Eleventh Serôs War in, the Thuvys clan was ousted from power in Selu'Maraar and the capital was moved to Aluwand. The title of Coronal was then adopted and Aolis Ildacer II was crowned as the kingdom's first coronal.

After the Twelfth Serôs War, the city was filled with a sense of unease as the Coronal Semphyr Ildacer's must valuable agents, the elven high mages of Serôs, left him to join the Nantarn Council. Paranoid at what might happen in the future, Aluwandans began preparing themselves for a potential future conflict.

## Alustriel (dragon)

Alustriel, more commonly known by her nickname, Triel, was a female faerie dragon that imprisoned the wizard-murderer Maddgoth in his own castle in Undermountain and made it her home in the, alongside her mate, Otto.

Description
Triel seemed to be about  tall to any shrunken individuals within Maddgoth's Castle, but was, normally, around . Besides her growth to this size, Triel appeared much as she did at birth. She was a very silvery faerie dragon, so much so that her scales had a vaguely reflective quality about them, and had thin, translucent wings of an aquamarine coloration that matched the icy blue of her eyes. Once she made her home in Maddgoth's Castle, she took to wearing a signet ring around her back right leg, matching with Otto as though it were a marital one.

Personality

Triel, though she had her bouts of overreaction, was generally of a cheery disposition. Any emotion she felt, she felt strongly, so it was difficult for her expressions to remain stoic when they so easily displayed her feelings. This characteristic passion extended to her whole body language, as she was known to flutter without restraint when happy or, when anxious, to rapidly hit her tail against things. She also had a slapstick sense of humor, but if ever her jokes led to genuine discomfort, Triel was just as quick to share an earnest apology.

The faerie dragon, however, had a sharp change in personality during her stay in Undermountain. While she grew accustomed to motherhood, Triel took on a much more draconic temperament, becoming quick to anger and very defensive, particularly around her clutch of babes.

Abilities
Triel had all the natural abilities that came to faerie dragons, including a paralyzing gas breath, and, given that she towered over any foes within the size-shifting field around Maddgoth's Castle, these were only amplified. She had also learnt sufficient wizardly capabilities to be able to cast a few spells, such as color spray, Tasha's uncontrollable hideous laughter, and slow.

Possessions
The signet ring that Triel wore was one of six, originally made by Chollas the Variant, that allowed her to activate the two-way gate between Maddgoth's Castle and the cellar of Chollas' home in Waterdeep. Given the magic she practiced, she also made sure to avoid destroying a variety of magical tomes and scrolls that she kept close to her nest, as well as claiming ownership over some of the possessions left behind by her close friend, Sandoth, a collection that included a cursed hat of stupidity.

Activities
Triel often ventured into Maddgoth's kitchen to forage for dried vegetables or spices. She found the place disgusting, magically burning some of the serial killer's leftovers and renouncing his filthy utensils, but the preserved foodstuffs she took to cook herself. Though she eventually planned to find fresher food up above in Waterdeep once she had taken sufficient vengeance on Maddgoth, they also served to supplement the food that her husband provided using magic. As an extension of these motherly duties, and to help lift her spirits, she enjoyed discussing the nuances of comedy with her children as well as telling them jokes.

When she was not occupied with her hatchlings, however, she also found the time to gleefully torment the trapped wizard, even, at one point, going as far as to fill his prison with water.

Relationships
Triel may never have known her true, biological family, but the bond she subsequently formed with Sandoth was like that between a father and a daughter. She was ready to depart Silverymoon, where she had lived with him her entire life, to stay by his side, and was wracked by such anger at his death that she swore to take cruel vengeance upon his murderer: Maddgoth.

For this man, and his homunculi, she had little sympathy, reveling in tormenting him or hunting down his companions whenever she got the chance. She insisted that, since he had taken so much from her and hers, it was only fair she return the favor in kind.

The only responsibility she held above that was the one she had to her mate, Otto, and their two hatchling faerie dragon children. Though she could be overprotective, anything she perceived as a threat to their safety earned her enmity and was met by her ruthless claws.

History
At Home in Silverymoon
Triel was born circa 1318 DR in the north of the High Forest. Her mother and her siblings, still in their eggs, were being used to nourish a young black dragon, leaving only Triel to be hatched successfully after a party of adventurers, known as the Moonsilver Corps, slew it. The survivors of the fight found her right as she hatched, leading to Triel mistaking one, the apprentice wizard Sandoth the Laugher, as her father. He and his sole remaining companion, Gulland Stonefist, took the strangely colored faerie dragon back with them to Silverymoon where they would raise her. Given her silver scales, her new caretaker, who so adored the silver-haired Alustriel Silverhand, named the hatchling after her, though he never addressed her by her full name and instead preferred the shorter "Triel".

During the intervening years, Triel and Sandoth greatly enjoyed each other's company. She grew to truly take on the role of his daughter rather than, as most people assumed, his familiar, with the two risking their lives for each other multiple times during their adventures. At some point, she also met and fell in love with Otto, much to her mage friend's chagrin, until sometime around 1365 DR, when she played a mutual prank on the pair that alleviated their jealousy. The three years that followed were some of the happiest in Triel's life, but this would not last long.

Move to Waterdeep
In the, Sandoth decided that he would move to the city of Waterdeep, and both of his faerie dragon allies agreed to come with him. Despite the other two's excitement, however, Triel could not help her trepidation. She was reluctant to leave Silverymoon and, by extension, her namesake, whom she secretly believed was the reason for all of their success. Even so, she found it in herself to look forward to living in a new city, where she could comfortably hatch her eggs under the protection of both her loved ones, and the three were off. Unfortunately, soon enough, her fears were proven right: Sandoth was murdered just two days after their arrival in Waterdeep.

The pair of faerie dragons had been out leading Waterdeep's City Watch on chases through the City of the Dead when, upon their return to their new home, they caught Maddgoth stealing materials from Sandoth's stores. Triel was so deeply enraged to see the serial killer's homunculi still maiming the corpse, bringing all of her previous anxieties to bear, that she felled the first of Maddgoth's creations, known as Brawn, then and there. She had injured the murderer all in the same motion, prompting him and his companions to run through their gate in the cellar back to Undermountain. However, Triel, managing to grab onto his cape right before he stepped through, was not so quickly satisfied, and Otto remained in close pursuit behind her.

Life in Undermountain
Though shaken by the sudden change in surroundings, Triel and her mate were quick to leap back into action, paralyzing Maddgoth with their breaths and holding off his homunculi simultaneously, until Otto was able to properly capture their friend's murderer. Although she wanted nothing more than to keep pursuing the homunculi, she knew her eggs were coming soon, and so relented to find herself a safe place to nest. Still, she and Otto found the time to commit one more cruel prank, placing a cursed hat of stupidity they retrieved from the rest of Sandoth's belongings atop Maddgoth's head. Not only was he trapped in a mage-proof prison of his own devising, but he was also rendered even more incapable and arrogant by the hat.

Triel was not content with Waterdeep and reminisced about the sunlight up above, but while she made her home in Maddgoth's Castle, she began to be satisfied with, at the very least, the modicum of protection it provided for her new nest. This nest she made, primarily, after venting her frustrations on the wizard's laboratory and recovering segments of parchment she could use. She destroyed large parts of his equipment and his tomes, most of all the spellbooks he had stolen from his many victims.

It was on Eleint 16 of the same year that her eggs finally hatched: one of standard red scales, while the other's matched her silver. At some point during her stay, she even began to sell off the various treasures Maddgoth had hoarded as a way to purchase Sandoth's property and keep the gate in its cellar hidden.

As of 1374 DR, Triel still made her lair in Maddgoth's Castle alongside Otto.

Rumors & Legends
After Sandoth's corpse was found, both Triel and Otto were being sought as possible witnesses to the crime, but, because of their disappearance, some Waterdhavians instead began to suspect that they were the ones guilty of his murder.

## Ali ibn-Dakimh

Ali ibn-Dakimh was a Calishite human enchanter and a member of the War Wizards of Cormyr in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He commonly wore wealthy attire comprising silks and velvets under a cloak that was azure or deep midnight-blue in color. It had a cowl that he used to hide his face. By his appearance, he could be recognized as Calishite.

Personality
He venerated three deities: Anachtyr, Azuth, and Mystra.

History
A native of Calimshan, Ali migrated to Cormyr, where he became a member of the War Wizards.

In the late 1360s DR, court wizard Vangerdahast dispatched Ali on a mission to track down various criminals who'd broken the laws of Cormyr, among them three thieves who'd been found guilty of murdering several Cormyrean nobles. Following certain rumors, in the, Ali tracked the fugitives to Waterdeep and Undermountain, where it seemed they would try to hide out in Skullport.

In the month of Eleint, he was a regular at the Yawning Portal after paying in advance for a month's stay in Cormyrean coin. He maintained a low profile, not giving his full name, taking a corner booth and monitoring all who came out of the well to Undermountain. Of his purpose, he said only that he was looking for someone who owed a great deal to Cormyr. In fact, his fine clothes and mysterious nature drew attention and the Tymoran priest Adama Miiralin stationed at the inn made efforts to investigate him, which he mentioned in his monthly report to High Priestess Seenroas Halvinhar.

## Allister Marnley

Allister died sometime before the  and was buried in the Lower City Graveyard, not far from the Durinbold Mausoleum. His gravestone was etched with a single inscription: "Here rests Allister Marnley, taken too soon." A single love letter was left on the man's grave marker. Shadowheart, a member of the Absolute Adversaries had known the man in her past, but the memory was taken away by the Church of Shar. But even with the memories gone, she still felt a twinge of sorrow over that loss, implying that Allister was someone she loved in the past, possibly a fellow Sharran who led a clandestine life and hid his faith. ## Alignment languages

The Alignment languages, also known as the Alignment tongues, were nine distinct manners of speaking that each corresponded to one of the nine alignments. Despite the name, they were not full languages, but rather shorthand codes of various words, phrases, and gestures that were divinely implanted in the minds of all creatures by the various powers of the multiverse.

Description
The nine alignment languages could only communicate basic things. These included expressing acceptance or disagreement, expressing emotions, and the concepts or dogma that were central to the alignment. For example, the lawful alignment tongues could express matters of administration and justice, someone of the true neutral tongue might state "all things are best balanced", whilst someone speaking in one of the chaotic alignment tongues might state "obedience to order stultifies". Beyond just words, all of the alignment tongues included some elements of sign language.

Most creatures innately possessed one of these alignment tongues, but for most this knowledge was only on a subconscious level. Unusually, alignment tongues were completely absent in the land of Zakhara and they were completely unknown to the korobokuru of Kara-Tur. Those who were conscious of their alignment tongue had at some point "unlocked" that self-awareness by recognizing congruity in the speech of those with a like-minded alignment.

Usually, only those of the corresponding alignment knew and understood alignment tongues, and thus they were a way to identify others of the same alignment. However, it was considered customary to speak alignment tongues only with those one knew. If one were to loudly speak it in public, or with strangers, those of differing alignments who could recognize it would see it as distasteful or offensive, while those of opposing alignments might consider it an act of hostility.

Notable Speakers
 Albruin, a sentient broadsword, was chaotic neutral and knew its associated alignment language.
 The Cat Lord, while in his human form, was capable of conversing in all of the neutral alignment tongues.
 All of the original members of the Company of Eight knew an alignment tongue.
 Deepchill, another sentient broadsword, was lawful neutral and knew its associated alignment language. However, Deepchill would only speak it if absolutely necessary.
 Sir Emelyn the Gray, a human wizard of lawful good alignment.
 All of the original members of the Knights of Myth Drannor knew an alignment tongue. This included Dove Falconhand, Florin Falconhand, Islif Lurelake, Jhessail Silvertree, Lanseril Snowmantle, Merith Strongbow, Rathan Thentraver, Sharantyr, and Torm.
 Li Po, a human cleric or shukenja of lawful good alignment.
 Lirana, a human cleric of lawful good alignment.
 Mervic, a human mage of lawful neutral alignment.
 Niatara, a high elf mage and thief of neutral good alignment.
 Pwyll Greatshout, the Duke of Daggerford, wielded a sentient flame tongue that knew the lawful tongue.
 Rairun Blackbrow, a human druid of Silvanus of neutral alignment.
 The Scales of Justice, a sentient +3 longsword, was neutral good and knew its associated alignment language.
 Shazzelim, a sentient scimitar, was neutral evil and knew its associated alignment language.
 Shazzelurt, a sentient dagger, was neutral evil and knew its associated alignment language.
 Silverleaf, a moon elf mage and thief of chaotic neutral alignment who operated in the city of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR.
 Sylabra, a sentient +3 longsword belonging to Merith Strongbow, was chaotic good and knew its associated alignment language.
 Thorym, a human fighter of neutral good alignment.

## Alabaster Staff (artifact)

The Alabaster Staff was a powerful and legendary ancient Untheran artifact.

Description
The name of the Alabaster Staff was a misnomer as the artifact was actually a rod. It was made of white bone and etched with hieroglyphs of exquisite detail, whose interior were inlaid with gold. A bronze band shaped like a flowing river separated the top of the rod of the rest of its body. The top was a piece of black amber. The Alabaster Staff was incredibly light, yet it had an indefinable momentum about it that conveyed a sense of consequence.

Powers
The Alabaster Staff had the power to command the dead, first raising them and then bending them to the will of the bearer. The Alabaster Staff was said to be powerful enough to raise whole armies of undead, and it was even able to reanimate the preserved body of a demigod.

History
According to legends, the Alabaster Staff was created circa According to Zimrilim, the Alabaster Staff was "some four thousand years old or some" by 1372 DR. by the Untheric pantheon on their world of origin. At some point in the past, the archwizard Hodkamset created a replica of the Alabaster Staff, known as the staff of the necromancer, in hopes of securing a reputation.

After the Time of Troubles, the Alabaster Staff was stolen many times by different groups, such as the Church of Tiamat, the bandits of Furifax, and the Zhentarim, but it was recovered by the followers of Gilgeam led by Zimrilim in the, thanks to the help of a thief named Kehrsyn. Zimrilim used the Alabaster Staff to raise the preserved body of Gilgeam in order to try to use the undead god as a puppet to rule Unther.

With the help of the Harper agent Demok, Kehrsyn was able to steal back the Alabaster Staff from Zimrilim by exchanging it with a replica made by the Red Wizard Eileph. Kehrsyn later used the Alabaster Staff to destroy the reanimated Gilgeam, with the help of the Tiamatan priestess Tiglath.

In the 1480s DR, around the time of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar and Shadovar invasion of Wheloon, Alyn Farrago was a frequent customer and regular drunkard of the tavern who told the story of the Haunted Halls of Eveningstar to his friends Olmer Twiggins and Zilar Fenner (and a tressym), and anyone else who passed by. ## Alia Durinbold

Lady Alia Durinbold was a senior member of the noble patriar Durinbold family of Baldur's Gate, presumably by marriage, and a member of the Parliament of Peers in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Alia Durinbold was a wood elven woman with a pale skin tone and blue eyes. She wore her hair, color of dirty blonde, in two short braids. Alia wore a diadem, a jewelweed coronet, and a long navy blue gown embroidered in gold. Lady Durinbold accentuated her eyes with golden metallic makeup.

History
In the, shortly before the armies of the Cult of the Absolute attacked the city of Baldur's Gate, Lady Alia Durinbold was a Parliamentarian of the Gate and voted in support of crowning lowborn Lord Enver Gortash as the city's Archduke. Lady Durinbold was present for Gortash's coronation that took place in the audience hall of Wyrm's Rock. Following the coronation, Lady Durinbold expressed her support of Enver Gortash as she trusted Duke Ulder Ravengard's judgement. Unbeknownst to her, Ravengard's actions were influenced by an altered illithid tadpole and enthrallments by the Cult of the Absolute.

History
The workshop was run by dwarf twins who went by "Sister Alabaster" and "Brother Alabaster." The twins happily whistled as they chiselled at the stone. They enjoyed sniping teasing remarks at each other. On Kythorn 28 of the, Alabaster Stonecrafts was visited by Tav, an adventuring detective who was investigating a series of grizzly murders. One of the dead bodies was fished out of the River Chionthar, wrapped in a tarp taken from one of the workshops on Misty Court. Tav found the open-air stonemason's workshop, and the twins told the investigator of strange happenings that had been plaguing the nearby Cliffside Cemetery, claiming that the gravedigger – Grom Skullsmash, was responsible for the. Or it were the ghosts if Brother Alabaster was to be believed.

## Alastar Bol

Alastar Bol  was the highest-ranked member of the Order of the Gauntlet stationed at Port Nyanzaru in Chult during the late 15 century DR, and was the faction's ambassador to that city. He was known as the Fat Knight and he also styled himself "the Scourge of the Unliving in Chult".

Description
Alastar wore fine clothes and was balding. While in Chult, Alastar had gained a significant amount of weight and a tan. He also sweated profusely.

He had once been a cleric of Waukeen

Personality

Alastar was very intelligent and wise. He frequently gave advice and expected it to be followed, else he grew irritated.

He was ill-suited to the Chultan climate and hated his posting there, which he found to be hot and boring, and so he often complained and wanted to receive a promotion with a posting to a different location. He had a habit of over-eating, especially out of boredom. He enjoyed sweets, and would use them instead of miniatures when planning tactics on his maps.

Activities
Alastar had been sent to Port Nyanzaru by the Order of the Gauntlet but he rarely left his luxurious and well-guarded villa in the Market Ward, which was close to the Hall of Gold at the top of Mount Sibasa, overlooking the fish market to the southwest. He was known to host lavish feasts.

Virtually everything he did was with the hope that it would make a good enough impression on his superiors to allow him to leave Chult. To aid in the fight against the undead that rampaged through the jungle, we was known to distribute holy water to Order agents heading into the jungle.

He was known to bet on dinosaur races in Port Nyanzaru, and his favorite racer was Mountain Thunder. On especially hot days, he did not bother to attend races in person, and instead sent his agents to place bets on his behalf.

Relationships
Alastor had a number of subordinates, which included Derio, Radwan Goldlust, and Gordo the Just.

He occasionally cooperated with representatives from other factions within Port Nyanzaru, such as when several of them elected to hire the mercenary Pozzanna Lordeaux to secure a stronghold within the Chultan jungle or when they rallied behind Hawkdriver Mazugulak to drive back an undead attack on the city.

He was an ally to the merchant princes, notably Ekene-Afa and Kwayothé.

History

Alastar was sent to Chult not by his own request, but by the machinations of one of his enemies within the Order of the Gauntlet. As of the events of the death curse around, he was in his late 30s, had retired from the church of Waukeen, and had already witnessed the Order's efforts to establish itself in Chult flounder, first at Camp Righteous and increasingly at Camp Vengeance. During this time, he sent Derio to investigate disappearances in the Old City connected to Viplo's necromantic scheming while he was under the power of a piece of the Dreamer's Amulet.

When Port Nyanzaru came under attack by the fiendish forces of Idrianne Smoke, Alastar saw the crisis as yet another opportunity to prove his worthiness of being transferred somewhere cooler than Chult. The merchant prince Kwayothé enlisted his and the Order's assistance during the siege.

Following the end of the death curse, Alastar brought together several factions within Port Nyanzaru in order to send adventurers to investigate undead forces stemming from the ancient city of Matolo. Privately, he also bid members of the Order to retrieve ancient texts about Ubtao from the ruins.

After someone used magic to impersonate him and gain access to Order resources, Alastar sought agents who could help unmask whoever it had been. Given the culprit's apparent familiarity with the Order's inner workings, he suspected it to be a traitor within his faction.

## Alewife

Alewives were a type of fey that had an affinity for taverns and other places with alcoholic drinks.

Description
Alewives resembled tiny elderly women with rosy cheeks and large smiles. They appeared to wear colorful coat-like clothing.

Personality
Alewives' personalities reflected the tavern that they inhabited, which normally meant they were friendly. When the mood of the tavern turned sour, so would their demeanor.

Abilities
Alewives had powerful magical abilities. They could create the effects of the spells friends and prestidigitation. Once per day each, they could also cast the spells charm person, grease, Tasha's hideous laughter, purify food and drink, and create food and water (but only to create only ale).

They also had the ability to possess any drinking vessel in their vicinity that had touched alcohol, animating it and sprouting limbs and facial features from it. This could be countered by a protection from evil and good.

Alewives also had auras that could compel people to drink until they passed out.

Combat
In combat, alewives often fought with broken bottles.

History
An alewife resided in the Growling Groghouse in the Year of Three Ships Sailing, 1492 DR. She would only talk to beings who were already drunk.

Appendix
Background
On Earth, an alewife was a woman who brewed and sold ale in the Middle Ages.

## Aldever's Street

Aldever's Street was a major road in Luskan's South Bank.

Geography
Aldever's Street began at the south wall of the city at Captains' Court, which sat on the north side of the road opposite the Hall of Warriors temple. It ran northwest to Captains' Close, where it intersected the Darkwalk. It then turned north between Captains' Close on the east side and the Ruins of Illusk on the west before ending at the city market. The road continued eastward as Setting Sun Street.

History
As of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Aldever's Street was a notably safe road by Luskan's standards, owing to the Luskanite soldiers standing guard at Captains' Court and the Hall of Warriors. Whenever Luskan mustered for war, Aldever's Street was the site of soldiers marching in formation as they made their way to the temple for mandatory prayers to Tempus.

Notable Locations
 Captains' Court, the palace from where the High Captains of Luskan governed
 Hall of Warriors, Luskan's temple of Tempus
 Ten Oaks, the residence of High Captain Rethnor
 Captains' Close, a walled park in which High Captains Taerl and Suljack were based
 Ruins of Illusk, the haunted remains of old Illusk
 City market

## Alec Carrathal III

Alec III of the Carrathal Dynasty was a High King of Callidyrr in the early 11 century DR. Like his predecessors, although he held the title of High King of the Moonshae Isles, he was considered to be merely a figurehead by the Ffolk outside of Callidyrr.

Relationships
He was a member of the Carrathal royal family of Callidyrr and was the grandson of his predecessor, King Erfle. His son and sole heir was Bayle, who would go on to be his successor. Bayle's eventual successor, Yolanda, was Alec III's grandniece.

History
Alex III assumed the throne in the, after the death of King Erfle. He ruled for eighteen years before dying in a fire in the .

## Alec Carrathal IV

Alec IV of the Carrathal Dynasty was High King of Callidyrr at the turn of the 14 century DR. Like his predecessors, although he held the title of High King of the Moonshae Isles, he was considered to be merely a figurehead by the Ffolk outside of Callidyrr.

Relationships
He was a member of the Carrathal royal family of Callidyrr and was the first son of his predecessor, King Benjamin. His eventual successor was his third son, Reginald, who would go on to be the last Carrathal to sit the throne.

History
Alec IV assumed the throne in the, after the assassination of his father. He created the Council of Sorcerers circa the 1310s DR to serve him and his heirs.

He ruled for thirty-six years before dying of natural causes in the . Two years prior to the end of his reign, the Great Druid of the Moonshaes, Brianna Moonsinger, was killed while trying to stop the return of Kazgaroth.

## Alyth

Alyth was a bartender of Swordsfall House, a shraehouse (a small restaurant/tavern), in the city of Silverymoon in the Silver Marches in the late 14 century DR. Around 1372 DR, she was a kindly woman of middle years.

## Alamather's by the Water

Almather's by the Water was a shop in Berdusk in the Western Heartlands in the mid-to-late 14 century DR that specialized in selling rare or unique weapons that were better suited for showmanship or worship than for actual battle.

Location
The small shop sits on the Gollahaer, the shorter street that crossed the Clearspring. It was fairly close to the bookstore Ondraer's Fine Pages.

Services
Almather's by the Water specialized in one-of-a-kind, rare, or unusual weaponry. Examples included cane-swords, gauntlets fitted with hidden dagger blades in the fingers, and barbed-wire whips. The shop also carried a wide array of trapped items, many set up to deliver lethal doses of poison, but the shop could not provide poisons by itself since the sale of venoms and toxins was banned within Berdusk.

Because of the nature of the store's wares, it was frequented by caravan merchants and professional killers.

Altymo Phaernphar was a maker of harps and owner of Phaernphar's Fine Harps in the city of Silverymoon in the Silver Marches in the late 14 century DR. Around 1372 DR, Altymo was a regular client of Chesmyr Morrowynd, a maker of harp strings, and they met to do business in Swordsfall House.

## Alakraena Xorlarrin

Alakraena Xorlarrin was a priestess of Lolth from House Xorlarrin that found herself participating in the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the 1480s DR.

Description
As a typical priestess of Lolth, Alakraena wore an elaborate spider-themed outfit, including a bustenhalt that bared much of her skin.

Abilities
Alakraena could call on a number of powerful divine spells from her mistress, including greater command, cometfall, unholy blight, symbol of pain, and flame strike. She could also innately see invisibility and could sacrifice a willing ally to Lolth in exchange for a powerful boon.

History
Around the time of the Darkening, Alakraena was kept captive by fellow priestess Teira Szind after being taken prisoner in battle. Despite this situation, they worked together as a team in the broader war effort, if only to please Lolth. In one particular instance they captured a priestess of Amaunator in the King's Forest, but Alakraena and Teira were unable to come to agreement over precisely how to ritually sacrifice the poor woman. This argument lasted so long that Cormyrean forces were able to catch up to them and rescue the Amaunatori priestess, likely killing the pair in the process.

## Alacrity (rapier)

Alacrity was an enchanted rapier of unknown origins found on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Alacrity possessed a +1 enchantment that made the blade deadlier and guided the wielder's hand toward the target. Its magics also boosted the wielder's intelligence and made it easier to defend oneself in combat, making it perfect for both wizards and swashbucklers.

History
The exact origins of the rapier were unknown. In the, one such blade was found in the town of Port Llast, sold by Haljal to the friends of the Church of Waukeen.

## Allice Bormul

Baron Allice Bormul was a Lord and a member of the noble patriar Bormul family of Baldur's Gate sometime before the late 15 century DR.

History
Some years before the, Lady Allice Bormul became a prisoner of the secret Sharran cloister below the House of Grief of Baldur's Gate. She was incarcerated in the Chamber of Loss and died before ever seeing freedom. Allice left behind her Last Will and Testament, written in her own blood, leaving possessions to her beloved daughter, Voebe Bormul. Her skeletal remains were still within the undergoing cloister during the Absolute crisis.

## Altispinax

An altispinax was a species of dinosaur.

Description
Altispinaxes had sails on their backs and a coloration that gave the creatures camouflage in the jungle. They had hundreds of long, sharp teeth, and powerful jaws. They could create a sound with the deep bellow of a lion's roar and the trill of a birdsong.

Ecology
Altispinaxes signaled to others of their kind through their bellows, which silenced the calls of many other jungle creatures. They attacked in groups, and were excited into a frenzy by the screams of their prey.

Biology
Altispinaxes were carnivorous.

History
Little was known about altispinaxes, save for that they were often sighted in Chultan swamps.

## Alienist

Alienists were spellcasters whose studies of the planes led them to the very boundaries of reality, to the plane known as the Far Realm, consorting with the unfathomable entities therein to ascend beyond their limitations and achieve new heights of power at the cost of their sanity.

Activities
While they could on rare occasions be found gathered in groups, such as to perform a special ritual, alienists were traditionally loners. Sometimes, at the bidding of some entity, they formed cults amidst the shadows of societies on Oerth and Toril. On the world of Eberron, many became members of the Cults of the Dragon Below.

Culture
Alienists held a philosophical understanding of magic that was distinct from other schools of thought. They believed that magic was no more than an individual's mind applying their knowledge and sheer force of will to transcend the crude boundaries of dimension, distance, often times sanity, and eventually time itself. To an alienist every formula, enchantment, and spell in existence represented hidden truths in the structure of the multiverse.

While most were traditionally wizards, alienists could arise amongst the likes of other types of spellcasters, including the likes of bards, clerics, and sorcerers. Though some argued that the magics of bards and alienists were incompatible.

Much like warlocks, alienists often consorted with and made allies or patrons of powerful entities. Though whereas warlocks might consult with entities of other planes, such as fey or fiends, an alienist concerned themselves solely with the nebulous entities of the Far Realm, powerful aberrations and elder evils such as Mak Thuum Ngatha, entities whose motivations were far less comprehensible to mortals.

Alienists generally fell into one of three archetypes: intellectuals, priests, and scholars. The first described alienists who were seekers of knowledge, the second those who revered their patrons, often in some form of cult or ancient religion, and the third the overly cautious researchers. While intellectuals were likely to plunge themselves into a maddening dimension or entity in their mad pursuit for greater knowledge, scholars sought to carefully avoid witnessing that which they believed mortals were never meant to see.

Within the Far Realm itself, this path of magic was frequently sought by the native kaorti.

Personality
An alienist's pursuit of the greater mysteries of the universe brought them plunging into abysses of madness that would fracture weaker minds, yet their mad confidence sometimes had a strong enough pull to sway others towards their vision of transcendence. However, interaction with Far Realm entities was ultimately corrosive to an alienist's mind in the long term, making them increasingly detached from reality, until inevitably their confidence twisted into a chilling mania.

Often their minds were assaulted with whispers from unknown entities far beyond.

According to some, because of the dark and dangerous magics they trafficked in, alienists were at risk of developing some form of madness, phobia, or affliction every time they learned or researched a spell, investigated formulae for potions or scrolls, or researched the ingredients or process of creating a magic item. These could range from melancholy and paranoia to more jarring conditions, like having their mind switched with that of an alien entity or perceiving sights, sounds, or sensations from the Far Realm. But most commonly, alienists developed a phobia towards a particular kind of creature, which extended to anything that resembled it or shared its attributes.

It was believed the mental instability of an alienist could be somewhat treated by means of a restoration or wish spell, psychic surgery, or an elixir of health. However, whosoever treated the conditions of an alienist had a small risk of contracting the condition themselves.

Abilities
An alienist often wielded specialized spells in service of, or in opposition to, the powerful aberrant entities of the Far Realm.

As a result of their connection to the Far Realm, any casting of one of the summon monster spells by an alienist would result in the conjuration of a pseudonatural equivalent—this meant they were largely incapable of conjuring other forms of outsiders. Even attempts to conjure celestial or fiendish creatures would instead result in the conjuration of a pseudonatural equivalent. However, they were capable of casting summon monster spells twice as often as other spellcasters. And over time even their familiar, if an alienist had one, would slowly take on pseudonatural aspects until they became fully functional.

As their studies progressed, alienists learned how to profit off of the enigmatic whispers of Far Realm entities, granting them access to metamagic abilities. Late into this path of magic, an alienist inevitably discovered an obscure means of achieving eternal youth, so absolute that not even magical means of aging could effect them. By this time, if an alienist were to die before they achieved true transcendence, their body would be whisked away by horrible entities, never to be seen again.

The apex of an alienist's development was that of transcendence, when their laborious studies and congress with Far Realm entities finally led them to becoming an alien creature. When this occurred the alienist developed some form of minute strange physical feature, such as a small tentacle, an extra appendage, an extra eye or organ, or a puzzling lump. Whatever the abnormality, it was not under the control of the alienist—the strange physical feature was likely to twitch, move, or otherwise animate in some fashion on its own accord. Some attempted to hide this beneath the likes of a hood or robe, though it was ultimately difficult to disguise. Some took advantage of their abnormal features, using their visage to intimidate.

An ascended alienist possessed some degree of resistance to acid and electricity, as well as suffered less harm from assaults of a magical nature. They were no longer affected by spells that specifically targeted humanoids and they were immediately recognizable as transcendent by other researchers of the Far Realm.

Notable Alienists
 Marzut Rensha, the Fifth Rensha lord of Loudwater, was a cruel and mad alienist whose endeavors into the unknown eventually led to his death.
 Zceryll, an alienist whose commune with the Far Realm eventually led her to become a vestige.

Appendix
Background
In reality, alienism is an obsolete term for the study and treatment of mental illnesses, coming from a French word, aliéné, meaning "insane". An alienist therefore was in fact an expert in mental illness, treatment, and associated legalities, not the one going insane. The change in meaning and association with the Far Realm most likely arises from its common appearances in the works of H. P. Lovecraft and the Cthulhu Mythos, where it is usually period-appropriate and correctly used, as well as a possible misunderstanding of it being about aliens instead. In the early 20 century, these terms were supplanted by 'psychiatry' and 'psychiatrist'.

See Also
 Aberrant mind
 Star pact warlock

## Alodia Drodeen

Alodia Drodeen was a drow cleric and eldest daughter of house Drodeen.

Description
Growing up Alodia were naught but a slave to her aunts, but with her mothers rise to matron mother of the noble house, she too saw her position rise above the rest of the family. As eldest daughter of house Drodeen she had quite the ambition of seeing her house rise up in status and power in the city.

Possessions
She wore an enchanted drow chainmail, a  helm of telepathy, and an amulet of the planes, while wielding an enchanted Scimitar, 3 hidden daggers, and a staff of withering.

Relationships
She was the eldest living daughter of Grazia Drodeen, and thus the heir apparent of their house.

## Alaithe

Alaithe was a maid working for the trader Blandras Nuin in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
She was homely and overweight.

Activities
The old woman was diligent and hard-working, constantly anticipating her master's needs.

Relationships
She worked for Blandras Nuin, and knew him quite well.

History
Alaithe was a maid working for Blandras Nuin in the mid-14 century. In 1369 DR, her master was sacrificed to Shar and replaced by Auvrarn Labraster wearing his likeness; she soon noticed that he was not really Blandras Nuin, and put kaurdyl in his morning broth, which he smelled. That led him to strangle her and bury her in the garden.

Rumors & Legends
Alaithe was later impersonated by Anastra Syluné Silverhand, who pretended to be her ghost seeking answers out of Auvrarn Labraster. She related an extensive story about having been taken in by Blandras Nuin in her old age after rejecting his affections in youth, when she was beautiful; it may or may not have been true.

## Ali-bin-Jafar's Textiles

Ali-bin-Jafar's Textiles was a shop in the Central Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Ali-bin-Jafar's Textiles was a two-story establishment of fair condition, marked at its entrance by a brightly patterned curtain.

Interior
The interior functioned as a warehouse for a wide variety of fabrics, threads, and rare carpets sourced from regions such as Kara-Tur, Calimshan, Zakhara, and the Hordelands.
Services
Alongside regular trade in textiles, the shop also served as a conduit for contraband. Bolts of inexpensive cloth frequently concealed smuggled items moved in collaboration with the Xanathar's Thieves' Guild and Misker.

Inhabitants
Despite the shop's exotic name, its proprietor, an ostensible “Ali-bin-Jafar”, is actually a Skullport native named Bertrem Thinwick, who adopted the alias to enhance his business appeal. Known for his genial manner and adept bargaining skills, “Ali” often succeeded in persuading even the most frugal customers to pay premiums for average-quality goods.

Appendix
References

## Aldluck Dreen

Aldluck Dreen was a Zhentarim operative in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
He was a rather large man.

Personality
While Aldluck seemed given to taunts and dark humor, he could also be rather grim.

Activities
He served as Calivar Murpeth's right-hand man.

Abilities
Aldluck was surprisingly silent.

History
Aldluck Dreen was at the revel in Warmfires House during the  in which Maervidal Iloster was to be killed. He taunted the scribe about the saisha venom slipped into his drink. Calivar Murpeth, his boss, sent him to collect Brezter and the others for that. Aldluck returned later with a number of men, one of whom was also poisoned, around the time Calivar had captured a woman by the name of Talantha he suspected of aiding Maervidal's escape from the revel.

The woman was actually a spell-disguised Storm Silverhand, who defeated everyone in the room. Everyone left over was captured or killed by other agents among the staff of Warmfires.

## Aliphar Moongul

Aliphar Moongul was a merchant and Zhentarim operative in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
He was handsome.

Personality
He worried little.

Activities
He was a trader in cosmetics and medicines.

Relationships
He had more than one wife, and none of them had met each other. He was also a member of the Zhentarim under Calivar Murpeth.

History
Aliphar Moongul was at the revel in Warmfires House in the, in which Maervidal Iloster was to be killed. He was introduced to the scribe Iloster by Calivar just as they poisoned him. He was argumentative about Calivar's concerns, but stopped short of second-guessing him, and was with the operative when they seized a woman, Talantha, whom they suspected of having helped the scribe escape.

The woman was actually a spell-disguised Storm Silverhand, who defeated everyone in the room. Everyone left over was captured or killed by other agents among the staff of Warmfires.

## Alysia Wass

Alysia Wass was a half-elven fighter, mage and adventurer, wandering the Realms in search of her long-lost mother in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
She was a half-elf with platinum blonde hair.

Personality
Alysia idolized elven society and was fascinated by everything elven. She would ask as many questions as she could get away with from those elves she encountered on her travels.

Abilities
Her great dexterity was her greatest advantage, as it allowed her to forego armor.

Possessions
She wore traveling clothes and wielded a sling and longsword in combat.

History
Alysia Wass grew up in Waterdeep with her human father, being abandoned by her elven mother who went adventuring. As an adult she herself became an adventurer, hoping to one day locate her mother.

## Albrazi Canal

The Albrazi Canal was a prominent canal located in the City of Brass on the Elemental Plane of Fire in 1367 DR.

Location
The canal branched west from the Great Canal in the Pyraculum near the center of the city, turned southwest at the borders of the Rookery and Iskalat, and continued through Iskalat until it emptied into the north side of the harbor. It passed under the Street of the Craftsmen and Street of the Vine along the way.

Description
As with all canals within the City of Brass, the Albrazi Canal was filled with lava from the Sea of Fire as well as oil which could be set alight.

## Alaric Wolfgard

Alaric Wolfgard was a monster hunter from the Reghedman Tribe of the Elk, who settled in the town of Targos in Icewind Dale in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Alaric was an intimidating human man of quite the size. He had a light skin tone and a bristly red beard. He spoke in a put-on brash manner but his eyes betrayed his emotional softness.

History
Even though Alaric was born into the proud and traditionalist Reghedmen Tribe of the Elk, he did not feel like he fit in and, when the opportunity arose, thanks to the Tribe's relationship with the Ten-Towns, the barbarian adopted a settled life in town. Eventually, though, he used his knowledge and barbarian strength to become an accomplished monster hunter in Icewind Dale. He knew how to fight and defeat various beasts, monstrosities, and savage humanoids of the Dale.

In Mirtul of the, as the Ten Towns were facing a looming threat from an ancient and buried evil, Alaric Wolfgard frequented the Wolf's Pelt Inn in Targos. It was then he may have met the famed Hero of Neverwinter, offering them advice on dealing with the many dangers of Icewind Dale as well as the Reghed Glacier. When the mysterious shaman Iceblood led an army of the demigod Arctus against the town of Targos, Alaric offered monster-slaying supplies to aid the town's defenders.

When the crisis was over, Alaric planned on traveling south, past the Spine of the World, away from Icewind Dale.

## Alura Durshavin

Alura Durshavin, also known as Pennae. was a thief and member of the Knights of Myth Drannor in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Alura was a sleek young woman in dark leather. She was short and slender with glossy black hair cut short in a "helm-bob". She had large, liquid dark eyes.

Personality
Pennae was a pragmatic, albeit cynical woman. Quick-witted, she was often sarcastic and blunt.

Relationships
As one of the earliest members of the Knights of Myth Drannor, Pennae held close ties with much of the adventuring group. Before joining them, she was also friends with Martess Ilmra, who also joined the Knights.

History
Alura was born in Arabel to a pastry cook. Her father was unknown.

## Aliana Meriadeth

Aliana Meriadeth was a druid of Chauntea and a former werefox adventurer in Raven's Bluff during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
She was attractive, still retaining a fox-like coloring and cast to her skin.

Personality
She genuinely cared for her friends and tried to take care of them, offering quiet wisdom when she felt it necessary.

Abilities
Amongst other mundane expertises, Aliana was a practiced cook, herbalist, and healer and was greatly acquainted with weather prediction, the lore of animals, and religious minutia.

More of note, Aliana's wounds had begun to close almost instantly, with her strength returning twice as fast during rest; this development worried her.

Possessions
She carried a spear and sling, wore leather armor, and had a potion of ESP.

Relationships
She tried to keep track of things for her fellow adventurer, the wizard Tanis Obereck, and made note that Taldar Everwind had expressed interest in her. Akira Iko, another peer and a warrior, took her aid for granted but didn't treat her as a servant. She felt very close to Serena Worthwood. Her final fellow adventurer was the dwarf Crakadan Earthdigger.

History
As a child, Aliana only remembered being raised by a werefox, eventually becoming one herself, and then breaking down in tears at the age of seventeen in a temple of Chauntea. There her curse was lifted by a friendly priest, leaving untouched only some of the elfin features that she'd developed due to the lycanthropy. She joined the temple, and later became an adventurer, eventually heading for Raven's Bluff and forming a party.

Before she was embroiled in the conspiracies of the Red Wizard Thule, her wounds had been closing instantly for a while, much like when she was a lycanthrope, which made her worry.

Appendix
Background
Aliana Meriadeth originally appeared in Polyhedron #113, with only two minor differences: the names of the agricultural deity she worships ("Merrinah") and the city she is in ("Lidah"), which are from a home setting in use by Robert Wiese rather than the Forgotten Realms. These characters were adapted for the Adventurer's Guild version of the Special Delivery module in 1998, and were reused for at least one more module in 1999.

## Alabaster orb

The alabaster orb was a powerful artifact and a wondrous wizardly implement called an orb that was recovered from the depths of Undermountain in the late 15 century DR.

Description
This orb was a decorative floating implement made of white mineral, such as gypsum or alabaster. Its exquisite draconic design was unique among orbs because it was once a decorative piece affixed to an ancient king's wall.

Powers
After the alabaster orb was reinforced and enchanted via magic to withstand even the hardest hits, it was given the ability to grant offensive boosts to the wielder. It increased the wielder's attack power, accuracy, and the ability to penetrate enemy armor. Additionally, if the wielder also possessed the alabaster talisman, the two items reinforced each other's magics, further boosting the wielder's power, and temporarily increasing their chance to deal devastating and lucky hits at the beginning of battle. The items' power to grant lucky strikes was reactivated each time the wielder killed a creature.

History
History forgot the identity of the ancient king whose grand hall was decorated with elements that would eventually become the alabaster orb and alabaster talisman, but these items were recovered from Undermountain and magically enhanced sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Appendix
Appearances

References

---

After the alabaster talisman was reinforced and enchanted via magic to withstand even the hardest hits, it was given the ability to grant offensive boosts to the wielder. The items' power to grant lucky strikes was reactivated each time the wilder killed a creature.

History
History forgot the identity of the ancient king whose grand hall was decorated with elements that would eventually become the alabaster orb and alabaster talisman, but these items re recovered from Undermountain and magically enhanced sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Alakinyon had his long white hair worn loosely.

## Altaira

Altaira was a young female Llewyrr elf from Sarifal on the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
She had yellow hair and almond-shaped eyes.

She was known to dress in white clothes with rainbow embroidery, possibly made by gnome slaves.

Personality
She was not brave: she was horrified and terrified of violence and was scared of the dark. She cared for her father and for herself, but had perhaps begun to experience some of the arrogance and emotional apathy that accompanied the long lifespans of her people. She spoke in a tone of voice that was equal parts fragile and haughty.

Relationships

She was the daughter of Lord Mindarion, an esteemed member of the Knights of Synnoria, who pushed her to be braver. She was first cousin to Lady Amaranth, the half-leShay princess of Sarifal, by way of her father's brother.

History
Altaira hailed from Synnoria on the island of Gwynneth. Following the death of her uncle at the hands of the leShay Prince Araithe, her father had committed his life to overthrowing the leShay rulers of Sarifal and to installing his niece, Amaranth, in their stead. By the late 1470s DR, Altaira was party to this plot, and joined her father at the conspirators' hideout in Harrowfast.

After her father suffered a grave injury while overexerting himself in battle against a drow darkwalker, he lapsed into a semi-comatose state in which he was barely awake or aware of his surroundings. This occurred just as he had brought together humans and fomorians to discuss an alliance against the leShay, but without Mindarion to head the negotiations, the talks collapsed. All the while, Altaira sat her father, fussing over him and applying ointment to his ashen face. When violence broke out between the parties, her father was roused from his stupor by the gnome Suka, and elected to side against his brethren. He instructed Suka on how to trigger a trap that would evacuate them and the fomorians to the Underdark, and although she was terrified, Altaira stayed by her father's side and was taken along with them into the darkness.

In the tunnels, she witnessed the resilience of the fomorians and heard her father express his frustrations with the failings of their people. He then resolved to abandon Synnoria and travel aboard to find something worth caring about, and offered Altaira the option to join him. The offer terrified her, and she wept. However, she would accompany him and the fomorians as they travelled north with the intention of finding a ship to leave the island.
