## Aldon Kendrick

Lord Aldon Kendrick was a member of House Kendrick and a distant cousin to High King Derid Kendrick of Callidyrr in the Moonshae Isles during the late 15 century DR. He was betrayed and killed on a mission to Sarifal in the late 1470s DR.

Description
He was emaciated, and had a long neck with a big head. He had a weak chin, which was topped with a meticulously curled moustache. He wore his hair in a queue with the braid tucked beneath a velvet cap.

Personality
He was vain, imperious, and naïve, believing that he was due great respect owing to his rank and family name. He believed in his own ability to inspire others, and tolerated no countermanding of his orders despite the fact that he often made the wrong decisions. He had little sense for when a situation required a firm hand or a flexible response. He was easily distracted by a beautiful woman.

Activities
Lord Aldon served as a solicitor in Caer Callidyrr, and worked out of a large stone guildhouse.

Relationships
Although only a distant cousin to the king, Lord Aldon was not without rivals among the many Kendrick cousins, some of whom wished to see him dead.

He did not bother to learn the names of those he hired, and he refused to dine with them.

History
Lord Aldon was born circa .

Serving as a solicitor in the late 1470s DR, he was confronted with the question of whether to hang a golden elf adventurer known as "the Savage", who had seduced the wife of a nobleman and convinced her to help him rob her husband. The Savage was a member of the crew of the Sphinx, and his captain, Lukas appealed to Lord Aldon on the elf's behalf. They ultimately struck a deal: the high procurator of the realm would strike the Savage's debt if the Sphinx transported Lord Aldon to the ruins of Caer Corwell within the fey realm of Sarifal to deliver a message to a group of human rebels known as the Winterglen Claw. Lukas accepted, although the deal seemed suspicious.

Lord Aldon perceived his mission to be straightforward and without risk: the Sphinx would sail to the island of Gwynneth and into port at Caer Corwell, where he would be greeted openly by Captain Rurik of the Winterglen Claw, who would swear an oath of loyalty to High King Derid in exchange for an indistinct promise of support in his rebellion. To the crew of the Sphinx, this seemed like a stupid and suicidal plan if not an obvious trap. However, Lord Aldon paid them no mind, ignoring their input and often sequestering himself from the rest of the crew in his cabin.

Upon arriving in Caer Corwell, Lord Aldon rehearsed his introductory speech before the party disembarked. They were greeted by an eladrin woman claiming to be a representative of Captain Rurik, and she invited the group to join her for refreshments. Again, the adventurers knew this to be an obvious trap, but the more experienced among them—including Lukas and the Savage—also realized that this fey entity had already captured them. They did not stop the clueless Lord Aldon from partaking in the wine offered to him, which left him catatonic and gasping for air. The fey maiden then killed him. She subsequently revealed herself to be High Lady Ordalf, the queen of Sarifal, and indicated that Lord Aldon's rivals within House Kendrick had concocted their mission in order to get Lord Aldon killed.

Although Lord Aldon's mission was a trap, it remained true that King Derid was interested in establishing contact with the Winterglen Claw, although Captain Rurik appeared to already know this when one crewmember of the Sphinx, Suka, encountered him a tenday after Lord Aldon's death.

## Almond

Almonds were a type of nut found across many parts of the Realms that grew on flowering trees.

Availability
Places in the Realms where almond trees were known to be cultivated included:
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold almonds under it's "Aurora's Larder" section for 3 gold pieces per, as well as almond oil and a jam that was a mixture of almonds and apricots.
 In nation of Amn, almonds could be found cultivated at Arnise Hold and served to guests.
 Fruits And More, a business in Ravens Bluff that sold a variety of produce, offered  bags of almonds for 7 gp.
 In the Vilhon Reach, roasted almonds were a staple of Turmishan cuisine.
 In the Sea of Swords, almonds were cultivated on the Moonshae Isles and the island of Mintarn.
Beyond Faerûn
 Almonds were cultivated in some parts of Kara-Tur.
 In Interior Zakhara, almonds were cultivated in the orchards of the fortress Sarahin and in the Garden of Delights within it. They was also available in parts of north west Zakhara.
 In Zakhara's Crowded Sea, almonds were among the produce cultivated on the Fortunate Isles through careful irrigation.
Beyond Toril
 Bytopia, where they were used by the plane's inhabitants to make an aromatic nutbread.
 On the world of Earth. During Elminster Aumar's many visits to the home of Ed Greenwood, he came to love the macadamia nuts and garlic butter roasted almonds that his host served.

Usage
Almond Products
 Almond brittle, a kind of sweet candy.
 Almond butter.
 Almond cake.
 Almond milk, a milk-like beverage.
 Almond oil.
 Almond tarts.
 In terms of alcohol, these nuts could be distilled into almond brandy or a liqueur.
Other Dishes
 Almond liqueurs were used to fortify sluth into zzar, imparting upon the wine its almond flavor.
 Dolmat, a west Zakharan dish that included almonds.
 Fryplate, a dish popular across communities of Northwest Faerûn and the Savage Frontier, featured generous helpings of sliced almonds.
 Halomonde, an almond paste condiment.
 Jack wyvern tail, a Cormyrian dish, sometimes used almonds.
 Mraed, a type of nut-based desert, traditionally used almonds.
 Wheels of redsharp were typically studded with almonds.

Notable Users
 In the lands further south and east of the Western Heartlands, it was quite common for ground almonds to be found amongst the spice pouches carried by travelers.
Businesses
 The Blackwater Stout, a tavern in Blackfeather Bridge (village), offered its patrons roasted almonds.
 Donder's Dancing Masques, an unruly tavern in Tharkar, offered its patrons roasted almonds.
 The Flying Stag, an inn in Voonlar, offered its patrons an almond and mushrooms soup.
 Hriiat Fien Pastries, a bakery in the city of Waterdeep, sold almond-and-apple sugar tarts at a price of 1 copper piece.
 The Lantern Inn, a tavern in Airspur, offered patrons a delectable dish of squid coated in slivered almonds, garlic, and red pepper.
 Maercrossan's, a riverside restaurant in Ravens Bluff, served sea bass slathered in roast almonds and mushrooms.
 The Moonstone Mask, a renowned festhall in Neverwinter, offered almond tarts and a blackberry-and-apple pie that was topped with cream and sliced almonds.
 The Plate of Eels, a restaurant in Hultail, served roasted chicken with almonds.
 The Raging Lion, an inn & tavern in Scornubel, served fried and stuffed snakes in a gravy of poultry stock and almonds.
 The Sign of the Forgehammer, an inn in Mirabar, served almonds as part of its curry dish.
 The Silver Taproom, a tavern in Essembra, used a dash of powdered in the simmering sauce they soaked their venison pies in over night.
 The Stag Triumphant, a sophisticated inn in Mosstone, sometimes served a stew of almonds & oysters at highsunfeast.
 The Swordsmith's House, a fancy inn in High Castle, served chicken cooked with almonds.
 Volodar's Stardust Inn, an inn in Ravens Bluff, served roasted quail with almonds.

Individuals
 The silver dragon Deszeldaryndun Silverwing loved to eat roasted almonds when in his human form.
 Duara, a Telflammar mage, was quite fond of almonds.

Trivia
 At the Cyrion Spice House, asking the proprietor if he had any ground almonds and offering to pay top coin for any he had, was the secret code for being allowed back into its illicit gambling den.
 Oil of invulnerability was noted as having a strong aroma that resembled the smell of burnt almonds.
 Tombworms and uttercaps were both said to have a taste similar to roasted almonds.

## Alustriel's sword pendant

An Alustriel's sword pendant was a small, enchanted pendant that could variously be used to cleanse and alter the shape of metal objects. Never losing its luster, this silver pendant further rendered its wearer permeable to most such material and, notably, was able to transmute the wearer's arm into something resembling the pendant's eponymous sword.

Powers
Upon Other Metal Objects
Sword pendants had a number of different magical abilities, all of which required that the pendant be worn against skin, rather than simply held. Three of these powers dealt with affecting metal items to which the ornament was touched. Firstly, and most simply, it could remove any sort of corrosive effect upon the target object, though any harm already caused by the damaging substance persisted. Regardless of if it was acid, poison, rust, taint, venom, or anything else, the pendant simply eliminated the caustic agent.

Transformative Effects
Into Weaponry
Secondly, an Alustriel's sword pendant could be used to turn a weapon that had both a metal blade and no enchantments into the form of whatever other weapon was imagined. This ability, however, came with limitations. The resulting shape, like its old form, needed a metallic blade that was not infused with magic, nor could it be more than twice, or less than half, as long or voluminous in comparison to the original. Beyond these functional restrictions, there were also aesthetic ones, as the exact appearance or engravings of preexisting weapons could not be replicated.

Through this ability, a typical spear could be converted into a lance. It was similarly possible that a knife could become a shortsword or a longsword become a greatsword. This effect lasted an entire day, during which the weapon could no longer be transformed by use of the pendant and after which the weapon reverted to its original state. After an approximate minute's rest in its first form, it was once again able to be affected by the pendant's power, suffering no adverse effects whether it be repeatedly turned into the same shape or into many different ones.

Into Carry-Globes
Thirdly, the last of a sword pendant's external abilities was another transmutative power, one that could affect any sort of object—not just weapons—as long as they were metallic and smaller in mass than a complete plate mail suit. By activating this particular effect, the pendant's wearer could transform the targeted object into a globe. This particular globe was a hollow one, with a distinct lid that sealed itself through the use of magnetism, preventing any gases or liquids from escaping the globe's inner space. There were limits to the size of the object created by the pendant, as with its other power, but these were no longer affected by the volume of the original item. Rather, the globe's size depended on the wearer's own physiology. Specifically, the pendant's upper limit was the size of the wielder's head, whilst its lower limit was that of their smallest fingernail.

Typical uses of this power included the transport of precious liquids, like water from a magical pool or dragon blood. Sealing the carry-globe with a flaming object within caused said object to immediately extinguish, and any destructive liquid inside it left the globe unscathed. This was also a longer transformation, lasting two days or until willed to revert. The latter option simply transformed the globe back, unaffected, but if the time limit was passed without the regressive command of the sword pendant's wearer, the globe disintegrated into nothingness, leaving any contents to spill.

Upon the Wearer
An Ironguard
Alustriel's sword pendants had only one sustained effect that did not first require their wearer's conscious command. This power made their bodies untouchable to metal, much like the ironguard spell, and so made them immune to prison bars, shackles, or even attacks from non-magical metal weaponry, which could only harm them through supplementary effects like poisons or extreme temperatures. Non-magical armor, too, simply passed through them, unwearable. This particular power did not extend to anything carried or worn by the pendant-wearer, and so meant that their items could still be trapped and torn by metal that otherwise ignored the rest of the wielder's body.

This ability also functioned differently if the metal had some kind of enchantment upon it. In that case, the offending object was stopped by a kind of field around the wearer's body, rather than going through them, which allowed for the use of the pendant alongside magically imbued metal armors. Weapons were also turned away, though they were capable of damaging the sword pendant's wielder according to the power of their enchantment.

A Swordarm
As its name implied, an Alustriel's sword pendant also allowed its wearer to transform their forearm into the edge of a longsword that was sharp and thin. It was capable of piercing armor and parrying someone else's blade without suffering damage, despite not being metallic in itself, as well cutting through hair, parchment, and even rope, as though the swordarm's edge was recently sharpened. It even behaved as if it had been masterfully tempered. Although, in this form, the swordarm lacked digits—and so was unable to carry or even grip anything—it could nonetheless be used to support the user's weight and, ultimately, it was even capable of hurting larger targets more deeply than smaller ones.

This particular pendant ability, however, required that the sword pendant's wearer wait four days between uses and, even then, would only last around forty minutes before returning to normal. If willed away, or if the pendant was separated from its wielder, then the swordarm faded even earlier.

Notable Owners
 High Lady Mage Alustriel of Silverymoon, both the namesake for and a user of Alustriel's sword pendants.

## Al-Ubaid

The Walled City of Al-Ubaid was a tiny city-state of the Whamite Isles in the Sea of Fallen Stars. It participated in a series of wild conflicts against the other nations and pirates of the Whamite Isles at some point prior to the mass death of the population in the .

Description
Al-Ubaid had a variety of colorful political figures that vied for control of the nation and the Whamite Isles. Known individuals were Anndoquat the Sardar, Felquish the Merchant, Dandarnab the Trader, and Count Clannano. It was served by twin legions: the Kardic Legion and the Neddic Legion. Anndoquat also kept a private bodyguard force. The common people of Al-Ubaid, loyal to their home, also joined in the wars of the Whamite isles.

Al-Ubaid was located in the mountain range that formed the border between Klocckbarn and Zagrus. Although smaller than its neighbors, it boasted impressive fortifications like the Gate of Odendog and the South Citadel.

Among the nations of the Whamites, Al-Ubaid was the only known city-state.

## Alsimur Droon

Alsimur Droon was a half-orc smuggler and merchant who operated in Urmlaspyr, Sembia, and the Eastern Heartlands in the late 15 century DR. He was a key figure in the Swift Thrust trade route between Urmlaspyr and Westgate, and often orchestrated shortages to increase the value of his wares.

Personality
Alsimur was well-known for his smiling demeanor and charming personality. He was conniving and under-handed, and sought the easiest routes to make the most money.

Activities
In his younger years, Alsimur was the leader of the Swarmers gang of street urchins in Urmlaspyr. He acted as a liaison between the urchins and wealthy traders. Sometimes he hired the Swarmers out as spies and guards, and sometimes he used them as as part of a protection racket.

As the Arranger of Shortages, Alsimur sent his agents throughout the Eastern Heartlands to surreptitiously create artificial scarcity through economic means, social trickery, and vandalism. He would then make a personal appearance to sell his wares exactly when they were needed the most. For wagons, he sold wheels, axles, drover chains, feed for oxen and draft horses, timber, and roof tiles. On the Sword Coast, he created shortages to sell cured pork. In Impiltur, he sold chains, cables, and wiring. In settlements around the Moonsea, he sold textiles. He hired adventurers to bolster his forces and protect his agents.

When in Urmlaspyr, Alsimur frequented disreputable eateries like Fishkillet, Silmur's Haven, and Mindle's Pies.

Relationships
Alsimur had many connections with merchants in Urmlaspyr and beyond. As the leader of the Swarmers, he had a large gang of urchins under his control. When he became the Arranger of Shortages, he put his follower Blarm Faronder in charge of all his Urmlaspyr businesses. His agents included the merchants Hrar Daekyn, Ongoleir Klassam, Marlar Tethnose, and Ulnar Keltur. When Ulnar Keltur was killed, Alsimur replaced him with Jharandarra Relhand and Scaulbrelle Arnketrel, both of whom he had a casual romantic relationship with.

In Urmlaspyr, Alsimur had no shortage of enemies, many of which sought to kill him.

History
Alsimur was born to the merchants Sonshra Longclaw and Ulivar Droon. As a child, he spent much of his time at Urmlaspyr's docks, learning from sailors and thieving. He quickly rose to lead the Swarmers, a small gang of dock urchins. Under Alsimur's command, the Swarmers defeated the Ironside Claw, Kalasper's Hands, and the Slow Smile in a gang war from which they emerged victorious. Having eliminated his competition, Alsimur rose to power as a "Thrust Lord" who controlled the trade between Urmlaspyr and Westgate.

Alsimur survived an assassination attempt, when a rival poisoned nearly all the food and drink at the Fishskillet eatery. More than sixty people died in the disaster, but Alsimur somehow escaped death.

Alsimur met Hrar Daekyn, Ongoleir Klassam, Marlar Tethnose, and Ulnar Keltur when he found them on death's door in one of his warehouses. He supported them back to full health, and with them invented his scheme to orchestrate shortages around Faerûn. They became his subordinates, and he left Urmlaspyr seeking greater profits. He left Blarm Faronder in control of his operations in Urmlaspyr, and Blarm eventually retired and passed the job onto the violent Tharn Olnar. Shortly after the group commenced operations, Ulnar Keltur was murdered in Scornubel. Following that, Alsimur added Jharandarra Relhand and Scaulbrelle Arnketrel to his scheme.

Rumors & Legends
Only a few traders had taken notice of Alsimur's scheme behind the shortages. These included Osilant Lharnree, Ilbert Nornust, and Presper Aulimont. Alsimur had some of these people assassinated to cover his tracks. The wise Elminster was well-aware of the full situation.

They could also be used as a +1 magical weapon, albeit only whilst still attached to the unicorn.

## Allisynn

Queen Allisynn was the first wife of Cymrych Hugh, the legendary Ffolk hero and first High King of the Moonshae Isles. She died at the turn of the 3 century DR, and was interred in a magical sunken castle known as Caer Allisynn. There, she persisted in undeath as a figure of Ffolk legend.

Description
She was an incredibly beautiful Ffolk woman, with long golden hair, delicate features, and rich brown eyes. She died young. In death, her body looked frail and her skin was so pale as to seem translucent, but she remained indescribably beautiful. She was laid to rest in a gown that was plain save for a hint of embroidery with gold thread.

Personality

She was a virtuous, gentle, and loving woman. She loved sunshine.

In undeath, she spoke slowly and warmly, although sometimes cryptically or sadly. She knew of events far beyond the confines of her tomb, and could even see into the future. She would sometimes pause for a long time, seemingly confused between memories of her old life and the people in front of her. She was said to grant wishes to pure hearted visitors to her tomb, assuming they did not attempt to defile or rob her castle.

Relationships
Queen Allisynn and King Cymrych loved each other greatly. They had no children before her untimely death, and all of Cymrych's heirs were from his subsequent marriages.

History
Upon Allisynn's betrothal to Cymrych Hugh, who was then the second king of the Hugh Dynasty of Corwell, her husband-to-be commissioned the construction of a magnificent castle of rose quartz to serve as her wedding gift. However, she would die in the, after succumbing to injuries received during Cymrych's legendary war with Kazgaroth. In grief, the king decreed that her wedding gift was to instead become her tomb, and he arranged for it to be imbued with magic that would send it safely beneath the sea but have it rise to the surface a few times a year so that his queen would not forget the sunlight. She was laid to rest in an open glass coffin adorned with plush purple curtains.

In the, Prince Tristan Kendrick of Corwell approached her tomb after having been shipwrecked. At first, she mistook him for her ancient husband, but upon realizing her mistake, she miraculously returned the legendary Sword of Cymrych Hugh to the young prince (who had lost it in the shipwreck and feared it was gone for good) and prophesized that he would be the one to find and elevate the first true High King to unite the Ffolk in centuries. Little did Tristan know that he was in fact the fated future High King.

## Aleine Whitsunday

Aleine Whitsunday was a cleric of Eldath from Selgaunt, Sembia, in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, briefly involved in a mission to establish diplomatic relationships with Myth Nantar in .

Description
She weighed  and stood, with fair skin, light brown hair and blue eyes.

Personality
She was the picture of patience.

Abilities
Even among her peers in power, Aleine's wisdom was top-notch.

Possessions
Aleine's notable carried possessions were limited to a ring of free action, a tub of Keoghtom's ointment, and two potions of healing.

Relationships
The paladin Sturge Whitsunday was her older brother, and during her holy mission, she wanted him to recognize she wasn't just his baby sister, but an accomplished young woman not to be patronized.

History
At seventeen, presumably in, Aleine entered the priesthood of Eldath through the Path of Silence, a yearlong trial during which she neither spoke nor heard a single word. Around the turn of the, she began dreaming of corpses piled on a ring of standing white marble and coral stones in a place she thought to be the Whamite Isles, which she took to be a sign from the goddess that she was to reconsecrate the ring. At around that time, her brother Sturge was tapped for a mission to find Myth Nantar and enter into an alliance with them; she informed him she'd be joining him, rather than asking to join him.

If she made it far enough to find that holy site in the mission, it would've taken her maybe twenty minutes to clear the dead bodies and reconsecrate the ring, which she'd likely do almost compulsively.
